## 44. Close the Residual Completeness Gaps §37's Review Found

Three findings from §37's review round 10 that are real but out of that section's scope. They share one shape: a guarantee is stated over a population, and the mechanism enforcing it covers only part of that population. §37 fixed the two members of the class that were its own (the stale bound rationale, and a completeness test looping over 6 of 7 record kinds); these three have owners elsewhere or need their own verification cycle.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-31. Sources: the Opus `kernel-quality-auditor` walk (whose lead SMP/bare-metal dimension came back CLEAN for §37 -- these are derivation-integrity and artifact-symmetry findings, not races) and the `parity-research-analyst` pass. The parity dispatch could NOT reach the web for the THIRD consecutive time (`websearch_offload_gate.py` R4 blocks the researcher agent it reroutes to), so its JUnit/kselftest/TAEF characterizations are recalled rather than cited; the finding below rests on repo code read at file:line, not on the blocked research.

- [x] Give the JSON artifact the same self-describing abort signal the XML artifact already has -- `suite-abort` record + host dimension
      On an aborted run `scripts/test.sh` injects a synthetic `<testcase name="suite-abort"><error/></testcase>`, so ANY generic JUnit consumer reds the build just by walking testcase elements. `scripts/utest-json-harvest.py` had no equivalent: the abort rode only as `summary.aborted`.
      - Shipped: `utest-json-harvest.py` appends one `record_kind:"infrastructure"` / `synthetic:true` / `status:"ERROR"` record named `suite-abort` after ALL producer reconciliation, so a `.testcases[]`-only walk reds a cut-short run.
      - The design review rejected the first shape: bumping `summary.total` and `reported.binaries_unreported` would publish a binary that never existed. Producer counters are untouched; the host dimension is stated as `summary.synthetic_errors` + `summary.testcase_total` (`== total + synthetic_errors`), written on every run so absent means "predates the dimension", never zero.
      - Appended AFTER the capture-model orphan reconciliation, so `suite-abort` never enters capture matching as a captureless binary.
      - The class it closed (as filed): a consumer following the doc's own worked recipe (`jq -c '.testcases[] | {name, status, time_ms}'`) SAW a smaller but internally consistent, apparently-green set on an aborted run, because the binaries that never ran were never published as records -- and JSON's stated audience is dashboards, trend analysis and bisect, the consumers least likely to special-case a boolean.
      - The originally FILED shape was a `record_kind:"binary"` record reconciled like any other. That is NOT what shipped and must not be revived: design review showed it would move producer counters to describe a binary that never existed. Superseded by the `infrastructure` + `synthetic_errors`/`testcase_total` decision recorded above.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §37 (item: "Decided: a refusal belongs in JUnit `<error>`, and the accounting was extended to match")
- [x] Extend the reason-width lint to the five LAUNCHER-COMPOSED reasons, not just the refusal set -- by ORDERED composition, not membership
      `scripts/utest-reason-lint.py` (§37) bound the `UTEST_RSN_*` refusal literals to their size tree, which is 9 of the 14 reason widths. The other five -- `UTEST_REASON_TIMEOUT`/`EXIT`/`LEAK`/`ISOLATE`/`INVALID` -- were composed from literals hand-duplicated at their call sites with no macro and no lint tying the two copies together.
      - Shipped: each fragment is now a `UTEST_RSNC_*` macro used by BOTH the size macro and the single `u_reason_<name>()` helper that composes it, so no fragment exists twice. Six inline composition blocks in `u_run_one` collapsed to helper calls.
      - The design review rejected set-equality as sufficient: a helper appending one fragment twice satisfies every membership rule and still overruns. The lint therefore checks the helper's ORDERED `u_append`/`u_append_uint` sequence against the macro's ordered `UTEST_LIT`/`UTEST_DIGITS_U32` terms, plus a no-bare-literal rule; the helper NAME is the binding, so a rename fails CLOSED (rc 2) instead of silently checking nothing.
      - Three review rounds then PROVED six bypasses of that check against this very checker, each closed and regression-tested rather than argued away: a macro expanding to a second append, iteration inside a helper, a fragment that decays to a pointer (`UTEST_LIT` measures `sizeof(char *)-1` while the whole string is appended), a redefinition of `u_append` itself, a brace inside a character literal truncating the body scan, and comment-stripping that erased real code between two string literals holding `/*` and `*/`.
      - A full AST or preprocessed-token check was REJECTED as disproportionate -- it would make the lint depend on a compiler invocation and a full include path to check five 5-line helpers -- in favour of a CLOSED SHAPE: only `u_append`/`u_append_uint`/`if` may be invoked, no iteration or jumps, fragments must be plain string literals, and numeric parameters must be 32-bit so the type pins the digit term the ordered check cannot see.
      - Eleven negative regressions in `scripts/test-tooling.sh` cover every shape above plus renamed helper, unmeasured fragment, and no-fragments-at-all.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §37 (item: "Bind the `UTEST_RSN_*` literal set to the `UTEST_REASON_REFUSAL` size tree mechanically. Every reason string must appear")
- [x] Publish a framed verdict line on the `task_create` failure path, like every sibling refusal does
      That path incremented the failed and never-ran counters and emitted TAP/XML/JSON records, but logged a plain `klog(LOG_ERROR, "UTEST", "%s: task_create failed", ...)` whose shape does not match the verdict grammar the host recounts (`scripts/test.sh` greps `<frame><name>.exe: FAIL|TIMEOUT|ISOLATION|LEAK`).
      - Shipped: the path publishes through `u_publish_never_ran`, which gained a `type` parameter so the record keeps the binary's own taxonomy label; the hand-rolled counter block is gone. `UTEST_RSN_TASK_CREATE` joins the refusal set + `UTEST_REASON_REFUSAL` tree (18 bytes, inside the pre-existing `UTEST_RSN_SHAPE` maximum of 29, so the derived name bound is unchanged).
      - `*out_verdict = 1` stays EXPLICIT at the call site rather than moving into the helper -- the design review caught that the helper does not own it and `u_run_one`'s caller reads it the instant the call returns, so delegating it would let a smoke abort be missed.
      - Was not fail-open before: `scripts/test.sh` escalates only when observed EXCEEDS the summary and this path made observed smaller, so the two counters disagreed by construction and silently.
- [x] Commit: `"test: close the artifact and derivation completeness gaps from section 37's review"`

## 45. Reader Leases for a Resolved Generation

§38 made a leg resolve through one atomically replaced pointer, which closes the mixed-generation READ. It does not close the generation's LIFETIME: the pin `utest_prune_records` grants is a snapshot taken at prune start, so the instant a leg publishes again, the record the previous pointer named loses its protection and a later prune may remove it -- while a consumer that already resolved that pointer is still walking up to its first `open`.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-31 from §38's adversarial review, which rated it [high]. §38 answered the read half honestly: `docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md` documents a re-resolve on `ENOENT`, which terminates because the record the CURRENT pointer names is always pinned, and immutability covers everything after the descriptors are held. That is a consumer-side contract, and §38's own text argued a consumer-side contract is the right shape for a window a rename cannot revoke. What it leaves standing is that a well-behaved reader can still be forced to restart through no fault of its own, and a reader that cannot restart (a one-shot pipeline, a CI step reading over a mount) has no answer at all. Deferred out of §38 rather than fixed inline because a lease is a new lifecycle mechanism, not an edit: it needs a grammar, an expiry, a crash-recovery story for a lease whose holder died, and a decision about whether a lease may block retention indefinitely or merely deprioritise it -- an operator-reserved design call, and exactly the class an unattended run files rather than invents.

- [x] Chose a lease FILE inside the record (`<record>/leases/<holder>.<lease_id>.lease`, schema `utest-reader-lease-v1`), bounded three independent ways
      - A pointer-side generation hold was rejected: the pointer is ONE file replaced by ONE rename, so a hold living there is unavoidably per-leg rather than per-generation, and cannot express two readers on two generations of the same leg.
      - The bound is what makes it a lease rather than a leak: `UTEST_LEASE_TTL` (300s) applied by the PRUNER rather than trusted from `expires_at` (a bound only the writer enforces is not a bound), a provably-dead holder reclaimed early, and `UTEST_LEASE_MAX` (8) leased records enforced at acquisition.
      - Stated disk bound is now `UTEST_RECORD_KEEP` + `UTEST_POINTER_PIN_MAX` + `UTEST_LEASE_MAX`; the design review caught that the pre-existing pin already widened it and the section's first draft understated it as KEEP + LEASE_MAX.
- [x] Implemented in `scripts/test.sh`: `utest_prune_records` honours live leases, and `utest_lease_acquire`/`utest_lease_release` ship the reader half
      - `build/.test-retention.lock` is the ordering primitive. Write-then-recheck only NARROWS the race (prune can classify a record unleased, the reader can publish and verify, prune can then remove it); prune holds the mutex for its whole body and an acquirer from resolve through verify, so no interleaving exists. The lock is dropped before the reader opens anything, so a prune never waits on a read.
      - Dead-holder reclaim is three-state via `utest_lease_holder_state` (`<pid>.<starttime>.<pid_ns>.<boot_id>`, parsed after the LAST `)` like `scripts/qemu-orphan.py:62-81`). Only readable evidence reclaims early; every failure to observe is UNKNOWN and waits out the TTL, because a wrong DEAD verdict deletes a record a live reader is reading.
      - Three false-DEAD paths were closed by review: a foreign PID namespace (a container records a namespace-local pid, commonly 1), a degraded `start=0`/`ns=0` sentinel the acquirer writes when it cannot observe itself, and an absent pid under a `hidepid=2` procfs that is merely hiding it. `UTEST_PROC` makes all three testable against a fixture procfs, mirroring `qemu-orphan.py`'s `PROC`.
      - `leases/` is created WITH the record and never by a reader: retention counts directories, so a `mkdir -p` resurrection would take a slot in the newest-KEEP set and push a complete record past the cutoff.
- [x] Added 21 regressions in `scripts/test-tooling.sh` (L1-L20) covering hold, reclaim, expiry, the TTL ceiling and both its evasions, admission control, lock contention, malformed leases, and every liveness verdict
      - L1 is the case §38's paused-reader test cannot reach: it ADVANCES the pointer after acquiring, so the record is neither pinned nor young, and asserts BOTH documents still open while `…03` is proven gone (a prune that silently did nothing would otherwise pass).
      - The reader runs in a `( )` subshell rather than `bash -c` so `$$` stays a LIVE process; the first draft's `bash -c` reader exited immediately and its lease was correctly reclaimed as a dead holder's, testing the reclaim path while claiming to test the hold.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §38 (item: "Gave the per-leg alias set a generation pointer, so a concurrent reader cannot pair two runs' documents")
- [x] Updated `docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md`: added the step 2b acquisition protocol and made the `ENOENT` re-resolve the fallback for an UNLEASED reader
      - Also narrowed the immutability claim the resolver depends on (payload documents and marker immutable; `leases/` an explicitly mutable control namespace) and corrected the retention bound.
- [x] Commit: `"test: hold a resolved run record against retention with a reader lease"`

**Test checkpoint:** a reader that resolves a leg pointer and takes a lease can open and read both documents of that generation after the leg has published again AND retention has run past the cutoff; a lease whose holder is gone is reclaimed by the next prune rather than pinning the record forever; `UTEST_RECORD_KEEP` still bounds the directory in the presence of leases. Test on: host tooling only (no QEMU dependency).

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/overnight/run-artifact.sh lease -- bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` | 1103/1103 tooling tests pass (21 new, named `reader lease: ...`, in the `run record: retention` block); `bash scripts/test.sh QUIET=1` | 27965 kernel + 17 user-mode PASS, since `utest_prune_records` runs at the start of every real run.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped `utest-reader-lease-v1` in `scripts/test.sh`: `utest_lease_acquire`/`utest_lease_release`, `utest_lease_fields`/`utest_record_live_leases`, and lease-honouring in `utest_prune_records`.
> - Ordered by ONE mutex, `build/.test-retention.lock`: prune holds it for its whole body, an acquirer from resolve through verify -- a happens-before, not a narrowed window.
> - Downstream: the `ENOENT` re-resolve is now the fallback for an UNLEASED reader, and immutability narrows to payload + marker with `leases/` a mutable control namespace.
> - Canonical doc: [docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md](../../docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md) "Step 2b: taking a reader lease", plus the corrected `KEEP` + `POINTER_PIN_MAX` + `LEASE_MAX` bound.
> - Scope boundary: the lease sweep classifies entries by type before opening, inheriting the check-then-act residue §46 owns for the pointer sweep -- filed there, not answered twice.

> **Accepted:** [H] A byte-canonical leg pointer can name another leg's record, so the pin -- and now a lease -- binds the wrong generation (reason: §38's pointer contract, and closing it needs a third document validated plus the consumer algorithm changed) -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §52 (item: "Cross-check the record's own `test-run-identity.json` leg against the leg the pointer file is named for, inside `utest_pointer_pin_id`, so a canonical pointer cannot bind one leg to another leg's record" at line 1880)

## 46. Inode-Safe Classification in the Pointer Sweep

The retention sweep §38 shipped decides an entry's fate from its pathname: `[ -L ]` or `! [ -f ]` removes it, and anything else is parsed by `sed`. A check and an open are two syscalls, so the classification and the action apply to whatever the NAME resolves to at each moment, not to one verified object.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-31 from §38's review round 3, rated [medium]. §38 fixed the CONSEQUENCE portably -- the parse is `timeout`-bounded, so a FIFO swapped in after the type check costs seconds and gets the pointer swept, rather than blocking at startup and wedging every later invocation forever. What it cannot do is make the pair atomic: bash has no `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK` open and no way to compare an inode against the object it already opened. Two residual windows remain, both requiring a writer racing the sweep: a regular file replaced by a special file between check and parse, and a special file replaced by a legitimate new pointer between check and `rm`, which would delete the newcomer. Neither is reachable from a second harness invocation -- `scripts/test.sh` holds an exclusive `flock` and nothing else in the tree writes `build/test-results-*.run` -- so this is a robustness item, not a live defect, and it is filed rather than fixed because the fix needs a non-bash primitive on a path that must still work when python3 is absent (the harness has a documented no-`python3` artifact fallback).

- [x] Decided: a small pure-bash helper, `utest_sweep_verified` in `scripts/test.sh`. No `python3`, no new dependency, so the documented no-`python3` fallback is untouched.
      - `rename(2)` and `link(2)` are the two primitives bash does have that act atomically on a directory ENTRY without ever opening the object, which is exactly the pair the hazard needs: `mv -fT` detaches the candidate so the unlink can only reach the inode that was verified, and `ln -PT` puts it back only if the name is still free. `-T` on both is load-bearing for the reason `utest_lease_acquire` already documents -- without it a directory at the destination swallows the operation.
      - Rejected the type-only alternative: it removes the parse but not the race (a dangling REGULAR pointer still needs its `run_id` read to be recognised), so it would have paid a real diagnosis loss for no correctness gain.
      - Rejected detaching EVERY entry, which is the reading that binds the survivor verdict to one inode too. It makes every published pointer briefly ENOENT on every startup for every leg, to close a window unreachable while `scripts/test.sh` holds its exclusive retention `flock`. Only destroy-candidates are detached.
- [x] Classify, parse and unlink through ONE verified object, so a replacement between the check and the action cannot be parsed or deleted in place of what was checked
      - Shipped: the by-name test is now only a CANDIDATE SELECTOR, and the binding verdict is re-taken on the detached inode. One verdict function per namespace serves both calls (`utest_ptr_sweepable`, `utest_lease_reclaimable`), so selection and destruction cannot drift into disagreeing about what is dangling.
      - `<record>/leases/*.lease` goes through the SAME helper, as required. §45's per-lease classification was extracted into `utest_lease_reclaimable` to make that possible, and `utest_lease_fields` grew an optional third argument: it binds holder and lease_id to the FILENAME, so a recheck reading the staging name would have called every detached lease malformed and unlinked the legitimate replacement it exists to protect.
      - Adjacent leak fixed while in there: `[ -e "$f" ] || continue` follows the link, so a DANGLING SYMLINK named `*.lease` (or `.<id>.tmp` staging) was skipped before any type test saw it and never reclaimed -- `leases/` grew by one per entry, the exact growth the reclaim pass exists to prevent. Now `[ -e ] || [ -L ]`.
      - A relink that fails is not assumed to be `EEXIST`: a non-directory at the name is the ordinary newcomer and wins, but a free name or a DIRECTORY is unexplained, so the object is left in its staging claim with a line naming it rather than destroyed on an `ENOSPC`/`EPERM` guess. Nothing ever deletes a preserved object -- the reap is `rmdir`, which collects empty claims only -- and an unresolved claim HOLDS its record, unioned in uncapped so it can never evict a record with a real lease from a saturated `UTEST_LEASE_MAX`.
      - Directory policy sits UPSTREAM of both verdict functions, in both namespaces, and is deliberately unchanged here: the verdicts define what is DESTROYABLE, and removing a directory the harness never created is new destruction, which is the opposite of this section's discipline. Filed rather than widened.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §53 (item: "Decide the policy, and say it in one place rather than in two upstream filters")
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §45 (item: "Implemented in `scripts/test.sh`: `utest_prune_records` honours live leases, and `utest_lease_acquire`/`utest_lease_release` ship the reader half")
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §38 (item: "Gave the per-leg alias set a generation pointer, so a concurrent reader cannot pair two runs' documents")
- [x] Eighteen regressions in `scripts/test-tooling.sh` (1103 -> 1121), both directions on both namespaces
      - The seam is the verdict callback itself: `utest_sweep_verified` invokes it with the STAGED path, which IS the instant between the detach and the unlink, so a wrapper racing the original name from inside it reproduces the window deterministically. The wrappers WRAP the production function via `declare -f` rather than replacing it, so the real verdict still decides every fate; starting a background writer and hoping it lands in a microsecond gap is a test that passes by not reproducing the bug.
      - p14/L22 special-replaced-by-newcomer, p15 regular-replaced-by-FIFO-mid-parse, p16 relink-restores-a-kept-entry, p17 directory-at-the-destination, p18 all four refusal/recovery branches with their outcome codes, p19 the same relink through the real selector plus reap ordering.
      - L21 published-name-not-staging-name, L23 dangling-symlink-reclaimed, L24 lock-free-release-inside-the-detach-window, L25 dangling staging symlink, L26 directory-under-a-lease-name, L27/L29 a retained outcome is restored AND counted live, L28 the snapshot binding itself.
      - L30 an unresolved staging claim holds its record across TWO passes (reporting alone is not protection: the object is invisible to the `*.lease` glob, so a warn-only reap let the next pass count zero and prune the record and the object together), L31 a leading-zero `UTEST_SWEEP_SEQ` normalises rather than aborting the shell mid-sweep, L32 an unresolved claim is held WITHOUT spending a capped lease slot.
      - Mutation-verified, because a regression test that cannot fail proves nothing. Round 1: restoring the old check-then-act fails 5 tests and NO pre-existing one, so the mechanism is behaviourally identical on every non-racing path; dropping the basename pass-through fails L21 alone; restoring `[ -e ]`-only fails L23 alone.
      - Round 2 found a REAL hole the same way: mutating the caller to ignore the retained outcome failed nothing, because L27 asserts the helper's return code and not what the loop does with it. L29 was written to close that, and now fails. Dropping the snapshot binding fails L28, the glob reap fails p19, the directory gate fails L26.
      - Round 3 likewise: reverting the reap's count fails L30, dropping the replacement-present branch fails L22 (whose predicate printed `live` but never asserted it -- the reviewer caught the dead assertion, not just the bug behind it), and un-guarding the counter fails L31.
      - The `timeout` bound stays load-bearing and p15 asserts it: the sweep must still COMPLETE and still sweep the thing that raced it, never that the race was prevented.
- [x] Commit: `"test: classify and sweep leg pointers through one verified object"`

**Test checkpoint:** a regular pointer replaced by a FIFO between classification and parse leaves the sweep completing and the pointer swept, never blocked; a special entry replaced by a valid new pointer between classification and unlink leaves the NEW pointer in place; the sweep still completes under the `timeout` bound when it loses either race. Test on: host tooling only (no QEMU dependency).

> [!NOTE]
> Invariant boundary, narrowed deliberately at the design review and worth stating so a later reader does not read more into this than shipped. What is guaranteed is that **nothing is destroyed that was not itself verified**. What is NOT guaranteed is that a replacement always loses: a FIFO swapped onto the name AFTER `sed` has already opened the original inode is parsed as its predecessor, so the pointer survives that pass and is classified normally by the next one. Closing that too means detaching every entry, which buys the stricter property at the cost of a transient ENOENT on every published pointer on every startup -- a real robustness loss on the normal path, traded for a window unreachable while the harness holds its exclusive `flock`. A late replacement is bounded, non-wedging and self-healing; a wrongful delete is none of those, which is why only the second one is treated as a defect.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/overnight/run-artifact.sh tooling -- bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` -- expect `1121/1121 tooling tests passed`. Host tooling only; no QEMU or kernel-side surface.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped `utest_sweep_verified` in `scripts/test.sh`: `mv -fT` detaches a destroy-candidate, the verdict is re-taken on the detached inode, `ln -PT` restores it if that verdict flips.
> - Both namespaces route through it: `utest_ptr_sweepable` and `utest_lease_reclaimable` each serve as their namespace's selector AND its binding recheck.
> - Downstream: `utest_lease_fields` gained an optional published-basename argument, `utest_record_live_leases` delegates its per-lease classification, and dangling-symlink entries are now reclaimed.
> - Canonical doc: this section plus the block comment on `utest_sweep_verified`; the invariant boundary is the NOTE above.
> - Scope boundary: the DESTRUCTIVE half only -- a replacement arriving after the parse opened the original inode survives to the next sweep, bounded by §38's `timeout`.
> **Verified:** 2026-08-01 | commit `4c7fa974` | 4/4 items | build OK | 1121/1121 tooling, 27965 kernel + 17 user-mode
> **Accepted:** [M] a DIRECTORY at a pointer or lease name is inert to the sweep and permanently blocks that leg's `mv -fT` publication (reason: predates section 46; removing a directory the harness never created is new destruction, the opposite of this section's discipline) -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §53 (item: "Decide the policy, and say it in one place rather than in two upstream filters")
> **Quality reviewed:** 2026-08-01 | Codex 6x (design, adversarial x3, consistency, perf) | 6H+4M+0L fixed, 1 open | scope: N/A (host bash tooling -- no kernel/boot/desktop/shell/userland surface)

## 47. End-to-End Proof of the Loader's Identity-Mismatch Branch

§39 froze executable identity and verifies it over the exact buffer `task_exec` consumes, and its unit tests prove the mechanism -- the digest is content-bound, the verifier is fail-closed, and an unfreezable entry becomes a counted refusal. What none of them can prove is the INTEGRATION property the §39 test checkpoint actually asks for: that the production `utest_loader_func` hashes the staging buffer, takes the mismatch branch, never reaches `task_exec`, and emits exactly one reconciled record for the planned entry. A helper-only test stays green if that call is moved below `task_exec` or dropped from the loader entirely, which is precisely the regression worth catching.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-31 from §39's design review, which rated the gap [medium] and recommended an end-to-end launcher fixture. It is deliberately NOT §39's scope, and not because of effort: staging a sacrificial binary onto the image is a `Makefile` edit, and `receipt_surface_guard.py` BLOCKS the receipt surface (`Makefile*`, the build script, the ABI generator) for the unattended runner. An attended session is the only thing that can land it. Two hazards the design must clear, both established while rejecting the reviewer's original in-place-overwrite fixture: ring 3 has no unlink or rename syscall (`user/include/syscall.h` exposes READFILE/READDIR/OPENFILE/WRITEHANDLE only), so the replacement has to be an in-place write; and `test-smoke-matrix.sh` boots ONE image four times, so a fixture that mutates a binary must stay idempotent across those boots rather than passing on leg 1 and diverging on legs 2-4.

- [/] Stage a sacrificial planned binary and prove the loader's mismatch branch fires end-to-end, with the payload's side
      effect asserted ABSENT so the test fails if the replacement ever executes -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §39 (item: "Added 4 `TEST_CAT_EXEC` regressions in `src/kernel/test/test_usermode_launcher.c` covering the mechanism over the real VFS" at line 1623)
      - Idempotence across the 4-leg matrix is the constraint that shapes the fixture: a fixed payload makes boot 1 mismatch and boot 2 MATCH (the freeze then digests the already-replaced bytes), silently inverting the test. Deriving each write from the current content instead keeps every boot a genuine mismatch
      - **PARKED 2026-08-01, blocker: the receipt surface, owner: an ATTENDED operator session.** Confirmed at source rather than taken from the note above: every sysroot artifact is staged by `Makefile` recipes (`Makefile:910-965` builds the directory tree and copies fonts/wallpaper/cursors/icons; there is no manifest or data-driven staging path), so a new sacrificial binary on the image is necessarily a `Makefile` edit. `.claude/hooks/receipt_surface_guard.py:59-62` lists `Makefile` / `Makefile.*` / `*/Makefile` as receipt machinery and BLOCKS the unattended runner on it, because `Makefile` is inside `BUILD_INPUT_PATHS` and is therefore what the run's own build receipts are computed over. Not a deferral of effort -- the unattended run cannot land this item at all
- [/] Assert the host side of the same event: exactly one record for the planned entry carrying the named
      `planned binary replaced` reason, `not_run` still reconciling, and a red run on both TCG and KVM
      - **PARKED 2026-08-01 on the item above**, whose fixture is the event this assertion observes. There is nothing to assert until a sacrificial binary is actually staged, so it carries the same attended-session owner and unblocks with it
- [x] Bind the loader's per-spawn state to the spawned task rather than to file-scope globals, covering
      `s_pending_test_path`, the two stage flags and `s_pending_expect_digest` together
      - **CLOSED 2026-08-01 by §50**, which shipped `struct task_utest_loader` covering all five of them (the four named here plus `s_loader_identity_mismatch`, which carried the same per-invocation contract). Nothing in this file reads a per-spawn global any more.
      - Raised as [medium] against §39, which took the release/acquire half and pushed back on the rest: `s_pending_expect_digest` carries the identical contract the pre-existing `s_pending_test_path` already had (documented at `src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c:27-33`), so binding one of the three in isolation buys no invariant. Not live today -- one boot-time caller, spawn-one-wait-one -- but the launcher is the only thing serialising it
      - **MOVED 2026-08-01 to §50, which owns the home this work needs.** "The two stage flags" here are `s_loader_stage_fault` (`src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c:109`) and `s_loader_reached_exec` (line 139) -- the exact pair §50's second item already binds to the child, and §50 states that item and its `entered_user` scheduler evidence "want the same home, so land them together". Implementing the four globals here would do §50's item as a side effect and leave it half-done, so §50's item is widened to name all four instead -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §50 (item: "Bind the loader-stage evidence to the CHILD rather than to two process-wide globals, so it cannot outlive its invocation")
- [x] Moved the timeout marker out of the `-(signum)` range: `UTEST_EXIT_TIMEOUT` (-6) is DELETED and replaced by
      `TASK_EXIT_UTEST_TIMEOUT` (`include/kernel/sched/task.h:1304`, `TASK_EXIT_REASON_BASE - 3`), with all five consumers migrated
      - Surfaced by section 39's re-adversarial round, which caught the SAME defect in the identity marker it was reviewing: a small negative status is indistinguishable from a signal death, so a binary killed for outrunning its clock and one killed by signal 6 reported identically. The identity marker had already moved to the reserved block as `TASK_EXIT_UTEST_IDENTITY`, leaving the timeout marker as the last one inside the signal range
      - **The deferral's stated reason did not hold.** It was parked on the belief that host artifact parsers key on the numeric value, so the constant and every consumer had to move together. No such consumer exists: `scripts/test.sh:3005` parses the NAMED `TIMEOUT` verdict token, and a repo-wide search of `scripts/*.sh` and `scripts/*.py` found nothing comparing the number. Nothing in the kernel keys on it either -- `timed_out` is a separate out-parameter set by `u_wait_with_timeout` (`src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c:1908`), and `test_usermode_report_reconcile` branches on that flag (`:4200`), never on the status value
      - No alias was kept (`kernel-code-quality` Gate 10, delete unused symbols completely) and no ABI churn: `gen-user-abi.py`'s `EXIT_STATUS_EXPORT` is an explicit allowlist naming only `TASK_EXIT_EXEC_IMAGE_DESTROYED`, so a new `TASK_EXIT_*` neither enters the generated header nor moves the fingerprint. `include/kernel/test/test_usermode.h` keeps only `UTEST_EXIT_SKIP`, because 77 is an EXTERNAL contract a ring-3 binary produces rather than a kernel-assigned cause
      - Pinned by two `_Static_assert`s over ALL THREE reserved reasons, not just the base: range (each below `-SIG_MAX`) and pairwise distinctness. Both mutation-verified -- restoring `-6` fails the build with "a reserved exit reason overlaps the -(signum) range"
      - Shipped a CLASSIFIER layer alongside the constant, on the parity review's finding: `TASK_EXIT_IS_KERNEL_REASON` / `TASK_EXIT_IS_SIGNAL` / `TASK_EXIT_IS_APP_STATUS` in `include/kernel/sched/task.h`, asserted disjoint at both block endpoints and covered by a boundary-walking regression. The reserved block made the causes DISTINGUISHABLE but shipped nothing to distinguish them with, so every consumer of the raw `int32_t` had to rebuild the `< -SIG_MAX` boundary by hand -- which is exactly how `-2` (SIGINT) and then `-6` (SIGABRT range) both landed inside the signal range. Linux's real answer to this problem is the `WIFEXITED`/`WIFSIGNALED` macro API, not its bit layout; this is the equivalent layer
      - The timeout path's status stamp is factored into `u_stamp_timeout_status` so the property survives into something testable: `u_wait_with_timeout` needs live scheduling, signals, a clock and remote termination, none of which a `test_*.c` may touch, so the assignment is tested by POSTCONDITION against a zeroed scratch TCB (clears `-SIG_MAX`, overwrites SIGKILL's `-9`, is not the identity cause). Raised by the test-coverage review, which correctly observed that the renderer regression alone would still pass if the producer left `-9` behind. Mutation-verified: stamping `-9` fails two assertions
- [/] Prove the timeout INTEGRATION, not just the constant: a deliberately hanging binary is reaped carrying
      `TASK_EXIT_UTEST_TIMEOUT` rather than SIGKILL's `-9`
      - **PARKED 2026-08-01 on the same receipt-surface blocker as items 1-2, but it is a SEPARATE fixture and must not be folded into them.** The consistency review caught this section originally claiming items 1-2 would close it; they do not, and cannot: those stage a binary whose identity mismatches, so the loader REFUSES it and it exits long before any clock runs out. A timeout fixture needs a binary that successfully reaches ring 3 and then hangs, which is a different sacrificial binary and therefore a different `Makefile` edit
      - What is uncovered is specifically the CALL, not the value: on the cooperative-kill branch `signal_default_action` leaves `-9`, `task_terminate_remote` is skipped because the child is already DEAD, and `src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c` is the sole operation converting that `-9` into the named timeout. Delete or bypass that call and both of this section's regressions still pass -- the constant is allocated correctly and the renderer still renders it. Owner: an attended session, alongside items 1-2
- [/] Make the reserved-reason block self-enforcing, so a reason added without its bookkeeping cannot build
      - The consistency review proved the current guard does NOT guard: defining a fourth reason as `TASK_EXIT_REASON_LAST - 1` without touching `TASK_EXIT_REASON_COUNT`/`_LAST` leaves every assertion unchanged and passing, the hand-enumerated range and distinctness lists omit it, and `task_exit_is_app_status()` then reports the new kernel cause as an ordinary application status. The comment claiming the omission would be loud was wrong and now says so
      - The fix is the reviewer's: ONE authoritative `X`-macro table generating the constants, the count, `_LAST`, the per-reason range and ordinal asserts, and the classifier bounds -- plus a mutation regression that adds a real fourth reason with deliberately omitted bookkeeping and proves the build fails
      - **BLOCKED on the receipt surface, owner: an ATTENDED session.** Written and tried on 2026-08-01; it fails the build because `scripts/gen-user-abi.py` resolves these constants by TEXTUALLY parsing `#define` bodies out of `task.h` (`TASK_EXIT_EXEC_IMAGE_DESTROYED` is in its `EXIT_STATUS_EXPORT` allowlist) and cannot see a constant an `X`-macro emits via `enum`, so it fails closed with "not defined in the translation context". The C preprocessor cannot emit `#define`s from a macro, so the table CANNOT coexist with the current generator -- closing this needs the generator taught to resolve enum constants, and `scripts/gen-user-abi.py` is exactly what `receipt_surface_guard.py` blocks the unattended runner from touching
- [x] Commit: `"test: put the timeout marker outside the signal range"`

**Test checkpoint (shipping subset -- the timeout marker):** scoped to CONSTANT ALLOCATION plus RENDERING, which is what actually shipped; the producer INTEGRATION is a separate parked item above, and this checkpoint deliberately does not claim it. Allocation is a compile-time property and is checked as one: `TASK_EXIT_UTEST_TIMEOUT` comes from the reserved block, and the `_Static_assert`s refuse the build if a reserved reason re-enters the `-(signum)` range, collides with another, or is added without bumping `TASK_EXIT_REASON_COUNT`/`_LAST` together. Restoring `-6` must fail the BUILD, not a test. The runtime regressions assert what a static assert cannot: that the exit-reason renderer emits a reserved-block status in full (`exit=` + sign + all four digits, terminated, inside the derived bound) and refuses a too-small buffer rather than clipping one status into another, and that `u_stamp_timeout_status` leaves a status outside the signal range and not SIGKILL's `-9`. Deliberately NOT asserted at runtime: the constant's own value, which would restate the static assert and verify only that the number was typed twice. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

**Test checkpoint (parked subset -- the end-to-end fixture, attended-only):** a planned binary whose bytes are replaced before its turn produces a named `planned binary replaced` record, its payload's side effect is absent from the run, `not_run` reconciles, and the host fails the run identically on the first and fourth boot of the same image. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

## 48. Wire-Published Facts the Host Enforces: Write Identity, Spawn Expectation, Chunk Bound

Three gaps that share one shape: the host has to certify a property the wire never carries, so it either infers the property (and certifies a truncated or vanished stream) or mirrors a kernel-side constant (and refuses every run the day that constant is re-derived). The fix in each case is the same -- the producer PUBLISHES the fact, and the host enforces what it was told instead of what it guessed.

> [!NOTE]
> Split out of §40 on 2026-07-31, verbatim, when the section-manifest complexity predictor rated the combined eight-item section SPLIT-RECOMMENDED. §40 keeps the producer emission budget (one mechanism: derive, marker, host acceptance, run ceiling); this section keeps the three publish-the-fact items. Both touch `src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c` and `scripts/utest-capture.py`, so §40 lands first and this section builds on the wire format it leaves. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §40 (item: "Emit ONE authenticated overflow marker when a budget is exhausted, then stop capturing for that owner")

- [x] Carry a per-write identifier (or explicit write-start/end records) so every write() call is INDEPENDENTLY terminable, and reconcile each write rather than only the owner
      Today `final=1` terminates one write but the host can only check that the owner's HIGHEST seq is final, so a concurrent writer can mask an earlier unterminated one: with a fork descendant or a second thread sharing the owner, writer A emits `seq=0 final=0` and then faults or is killed before its tail, writer B emits `seq=1 final=1`, and the sequence is contiguous with a final highest record -- so the host certifies an owner whose first write was truncated. No record is missing, so frame-level record counting cannot see it either, and no host-side reconciler can close it because the wire format carries no write identity. Found by the §36 round-2 adversarial review. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §36 (item: "Reconcile captured-versus-declared bytes FAIL-CLOSED, now that both sides count exactly the same bytes")
      - SHIPPED as `wr=<id>` on every `[UTEST-CAPTURE]` record, drawn as the sequence number of that write's FIRST emitted chunk and held in the call-local `struct utest_capture_ctx` (`_wid`/`_has_wid`), so no new counter and no new task field: seq is already unique per owner under `s_capture_budget_lock`. `_check_writes` (`scripts/utest-capture.py`) groups by `(owner, wr)` and requires one `final=1` at each group's last member, plus `wr == min(group)` so records cannot be relabelled into a write the producer never emitted. `UTEST_CAPTURE_FIXED` grew by the field, so `UTEST_CAPTURE_CHUNK_MAX` re-derives 46 -> 42 and `UTEST_CAPTURE_OWNER_WIRE_MAX` rises 512 -> 640 KiB to keep covering the same 64 KiB raw allowance.
      - RESIDUAL, owned by §54: a write killed mid-syscall BEFORE its first chunk flushed (under one chunk of payload) emits nothing, so it creates no group to leave open. Both live callers (`syscall.c:99-134`, `nt_syscall.c:394-404`) reach `test_usermode_capture_end()` unconditionally on the normal path, so this needs a task death inside the write loop. Closing it needs an emitted write-start event per write() call, which would roughly double capture serial traffic for the whole suite -- hence a separate section with its own cost decision. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §54 (item: "Detect a write abandoned before its first chunk ever reached the wire")
      - §40's `[UTEST-CAPTURE-OVER]` terminator INHERITS this exposure and slightly widens it, which is an accepted residual owned here rather than a §40 defect. Its adversarial review rated the widening [high] and recommended keeping a budget stop fail-closed until write identity lands; that was REJECTED with evidence, because refusing a budgeted stop is verbatim the `capture_unterminated` false-red §40 exists to close. What the marker relaxes is narrow: without it the highest seq must be `final=1`, so at least the LAST write was known-terminated; with it, the write the budget cut is legitimately open. The masking of an EARLIER writer's truncated write is unchanged and pre-existing -- it is this item.
      - RESOLVED differently from the wording above, which proposed requiring every write below the terminator to be complete. That check would have REFUSED a legitimate budget stop: the producer latches the whole OWNER (`utest_capture_stopped`), so every write in flight at that instant stops mid-stream, not only the one that tripped it -- deterministically, for any binary with a fork descendant or second thread writing concurrently. So a validated marker keeps exempting its owner from TERMINATION exactly as it did before, and the grouping checks (`wr == min(group)`, at most one `final`) still apply. Nothing is lost: a budget-stopped owner already publishes `budget_stop` and its payload is declared bounded rather than complete, so per-write completeness would add no claim the artifact makes anyway. Pinned by the `over_two_open` fixture (two writes open across a stop reconciles green).
- [x] Mark which testcases were SPAWNED (`capture_expected`) so a vanished capture channel cannot read as success
      The §36 population check runs capture-to-testcase only, because "a testcase with no captured output" is legal for infrastructure rows, skip blocks and binaries that never launched. The consequence is that a producer regression which stops arming capture (or stops emitting `[UTEST-CAPTURE-BEGIN]`) yields an EMPTY model that both artifacts accept as ordinary output-free testcases, and the frame count still reconciles because those records were never produced. Closing it needs the artifact schema to distinguish "spawned, so capture was expected" from "never spawned" -- inferring it host-side would refuse legitimate launch-failure records. Both renderers must then require capture for the expected subset, identically. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §36 (item: "Reconcile captured-versus-declared bytes FAIL-CLOSED, now that both sides count exactly the same bytes")
      - SHIPPED as `[UTEST-CAPTURE-EXPECT] owner=<pid> name=<n>`, emitted by `u_spawn_one` on the LAUNCHER's spawn path -- deliberately a different code path from the arming that emits BEGIN inside `task_create_internal` (`task.c:915-925`), which is why losing the arming is now visible instead of silently emptying the model. `_check_expectations` refuses `capture_channel_missing` / `capture_expect_mismatch`. Both renderers inherit it for free: the refusal lives in the ONE shared model builder they already consume, so they cannot diverge.
      - The expectation set is itself falsifiable. `[UTEST-FRAME-END]` now carries `spawned=<n>`, raised in `u_run_one` (not beside the record emission), and the host requires exactly that many distinct EXPECT owners -- so deleting either half alone refuses. A terminator that omits the census is refused rather than read as "no terminator", which the adversarial round found was the way to make the whole census evaporate.
      - The invariant runs BOTH ways. Requiring only EXPECT -> BEGIN was fail-open: dropping an owner's expectation AND its spawn increment together leaves a self-consistent census (`spawned=0` beside a bound owner still publishing its capture as trustworthy). `capture_unexpected_channel` refuses a channel the run never announced, per owner, so the diagnosis names the binary. Proven against a real slice: `spawned=17` with 17 EXPECT records reconciles, and dropping one EXPECT line refuses with `owner=9 ('test_syscall.exe') armed a capture channel that the run never announced as spawned`.
      - The arming postcondition is a pure predicate (`u_capture_armed_for`) with a test seam, because a healthy spawn only ever exercises its armed branch -- the NULL, inactive and wrong-owner branches are covered by assertions over scratch task slots, not by any live run.
      - Never-spawned rows stay green by construction: no EXPECT record is emitted for them, so there is nothing to reconcile -- which is why the expectation is published rather than inferred host-side.
- [x] Publish the producer's chunk bound on the wire so the host can enforce it without a mirrored constant
      `UTEST_CAPTURE_CHUNK_MAX` derives to 46 raw bytes from `UTEST_RECORD_LINE_MAX`, `UTEST_CAPTURE_FIXED` and `UTEST_CAPTURE_ESCAPE_EXPANSION`, so the host cannot enforce it today: a hardcoded 46 in `scripts/utest-capture.py` would silently refuse EVERY run the day that derivation changes, which is a worse failure than the over-long chunk it rejects. Carry the bound in the `[UTEST-FRAME]` announcement (or the capture BEGIN record) and have the host reject any declared or decoded chunk above the announced value. Found by the §36 consistency review. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §36 (item: "Reconcile captured-versus-declared bytes FAIL-CLOSED, now that both sides count exactly the same bytes")
      - SHIPPED on `[UTEST-CAPTURE-BEGIN]` as `chunk_max=<n>`, not on `[UTEST-FRAME]`. The design review recommended the frame line as a run-wide protocol fact; REJECTED with evidence, because `scripts/utest-frame.py:98-101` owns the framing records and a capture-protocol field inside them splits one record across two parsers. BEGIN is also exactly co-extensive with reconcilability: a chunk whose owner has no BEGIN is already `capture_unbound_owner`, so declaration and use have identical lifetime.
      - `_check_chunk_bounds` refuses `capture_chunk_oversize` above the announced value, `capture_chunk_max_conflict` when announcements disagree, and `capture_chunk_max_invalid` on a zero bound. A real run announces 42 (the value the `wr=` field's own cost re-derived it to), and the `rederived` fixture proves a different announced bound reconciles green with NO host edit -- which is the whole point of publishing it.
- [x] Commit: `"test: publish write identity, spawn expectation and the chunk bound on the capture wire"`

**Test checkpoint:** a fixture where one write is abandoned mid-stream and a second write from the same owner completes is REFUSED rather than certified; an empty capture model fails a run containing spawned testcases while leaving never-spawned and refusal records green; a record whose decoded chunk exceeds the ANNOUNCED bound is refused, and re-deriving `UTEST_CAPTURE_CHUNK_MAX` changes the announced value without any host edit. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` (31 new fixtures: the capture set -- `mask`, `interleave`, `over_two_open`, the write-identity refusals, the chunk-bound cases including the re-announce forgery, the expectation cases in both directions, the loss-precedence pair and the shape-precedence boundary -- plus the framing set for the census: the obsolete two-field terminator and four non-producer census shapes; 1152/1152 expected) and `scripts/debug/kernel/run-exec-tests.bat` / `bash scripts/test.sh SUITE=exec` for the three kernel-side tests (`UTEST: write identity spans one write only`, `UTEST: capture BEGIN announces the enforced chunk bound`, `UTEST: arming postcondition covers every branch`).

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped three published facts on the capture wire: `wr=` write identity on every chunk, `[UTEST-CAPTURE-EXPECT]` per spawned binary with a `spawned=` count on `[UTEST-FRAME-END]`, and `chunk_max=` on `[UTEST-CAPTURE-BEGIN]`.
> - Producer side is `src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c` plus two `struct utest_capture_ctx` fields; the host enforcement is three new checkers in `scripts/utest-capture.py` (`_check_writes`, `_check_expectations`, `_check_chunk_bounds`).
> - Both artifact renderers inherit every new refusal without changes, because they consume the one shared model this module publishes; the model's own JSON shape is unchanged.
> - `UTEST_CAPTURE_CHUNK_MAX` re-derives 46 -> 42 under the wider record, so `UTEST_CAPTURE_OWNER_WIRE_MAX` rose 512 -> 640 KiB to keep covering the same 64 KiB raw allowance.
> - Verified end-to-end on a real run: `chunk_max=42`, `spawned=17`, 17 EXPECT records, model `ok: true`; dropping one EXPECT line from that slice refuses and names the binary.
> - Refusal PRECEDENCE is now stated in `_reconcile` and pinned by fixtures: within reconciliation a producer-declared loss outranks the expectation contract and the chunk bound; record-SHAPE faults stay ahead of both, where they always were.
> - Review tightened `scripts/utest-frame.py`'s terminator grammar to canonical+anchored, so the census cannot be evaded on the TAP-only path where the capture model never runs.

> **Accepted:** [H] a write killed mid-syscall before its first chunk flushed emits nothing, so it creates no group to leave open (reason: visibility needs an emitted write-start event, which roughly doubles capture serial traffic -- a cost decision of its own) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §54 (item: "Detect a write abandoned before its first chunk ever reached the wire" at line 2255)

## 49. Run-Boundary Fence for Emitters That Outlive Their Binary

The launcher waits on the top-level pid only (`u_run_one`), but a `fork()` descendant inherits capture ownership unchanged (`src/kernel/sched/task.c:2459`), so a descendant can outlive the binary it belongs to and keep emitting into whatever run is current when it next reaches klog. `test_usermode_run()` is documented as safe to call repeatedly and `u_frame_begin` reuses the framing nonce, so a stale record is not merely late -- it is AUTHENTICATED and gets counted in a run that never produced it, perturbing `s_frame_records`, the owner/sequence reconciliation, and any run-scoped accounting. Two shipped binaries fork (`user/test/test_process.c`, `user/test/test_faultinject.c`).

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-07-31 from §40's round-2 re-adversarial review, which rated it [medium]. §40 closed the half its own mechanism owns -- a capture claim charged to run N is dropped rather than emitted into run N+1, via a run generation bumped in the budget reset and checked immediately before emission -- and deliberately did NOT widen into this one: the residual predates the budget, affects record kinds the budget never touches, and a real fix is a framing-layer lifetime change (drain in-flight emitters, or reap the whole captured descendant tree, before `u_frame_end`). Doing that inside a budget section would have coupled two unrelated mechanisms. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §40 (item: "Added a run-wide budget (`UTEST_CAPTURE_RUN_RECORD_BUDGET`, 22796) so N binaries each under the per-owner cap cannot sum past the run ceiling")

- [x] Reap or fence the whole CAPTURED DESCENDANT TREE at the run boundary, not just the top-level pid
      - Shipped as BOTH shapes the reviews named, because neither alone is a fence. Admission is sealed at the run boundary (`s_capture_sealed`, a new `UTEST_CAP_SEALED` verdict decided inside `u_capture_decide`), then the outstanding claims are drained (`u_capture_drain`, bounded by `UTEST_CAPTURE_DRAIN_MS`), then the descendant tree is reaped (`u_capture_reap_tree` from `u_run_one`, before the owner's own `task_cleanup`).
      - The seal, not the task walk, is what closes the TOCTOU. A per-task pass cannot fence a write already past `test_usermode_capture_start`, which has latched `ctx->_active` / `ctx->_owner` and never rereads the task fields, nor a descendant forked while the walk yields. Every emitter must still call `u_capture_claim`, so one flag read under the lock that already linearizes claims stops all of them. The design review rated the walk-only shape [high] for exactly this.
      - Reservations are MONOTONIC counters (`s_capture_admitted` / `s_capture_completed` / `s_capture_forgiven`), never per-run reset: a run-scoped reset underflows when a claim admitted in run N decrements a counter run N+1 already zeroed. `u_capture_pending` saturates so a completion arriving after its own write-off reads zero rather than four billion.
      - The reap runs to a FIXED POINT (`UTEST_CAPTURE_REAP_ROUNDS`) because a descendant can fork while the walk yields, and it now calls `task_cleanup` per descendant, highest pid first -- `task_terminate_remote` only marks DEAD, so stacks, CR3, PEB/TEB and the handle table were leaking across repeated runs. New reason `TASK_EXIT_UTEST_REAPED` (task.h) rather than reusing the timeout reason: a descendant never had a wall clock of its own. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §40 (item: "Added a run-wide budget (`UTEST_CAPTURE_RUN_RECORD_BUDGET`, 22796) so N binaries each under the per-owner cap cannot sum past the run ceiling")
- [x] Make a stale record's arrival DIAGNOSABLE rather than merely counted, so the run that inherits it is not the one that fails
      - Reported in the ORIGINATING run, before its own terminator, as `[UTEST-CAPTURE-PENDING] run=N pending=K` -- not as a record in the run that inherits it. A stale arrival announced in the successor cannot make the guilty run fail (it has closed), so it would only contaminate an innocent one; and `u_frame_begin` resets the record count and bumps the generation BEFORE publishing `s_frame_open`, so a diagnostic emitted there would count toward the new run while sitting outside the parser's slice for it.
      - `[UTEST-CAPTURE-UNREAPED] owner=P live=N` reports a producer forking faster than the reap's round cap. `scripts/utest-capture.py` parses both and refuses the run semantically (`capture_pending_records`, `capture_unreaped_descendants`); an unrecognized capture-family record would otherwise refuse as `capture_malformed_record`, reporting a working producer as corruption.
      - REACH, stated honestly: that refusal only lands where the capture model is actually built, and `scripts/test.sh` gates the whole reconciliation block on `XML=1` or `JSON=1` (both default 0), so a plain `bash scripts/test.sh` never runs the host checker and both records travel as `LOG_WARN` only. That is pre-existing and applies to EVERY capture refusal, not these two; §59 owns it. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §59 (item: "Reconcile the capture model on every complete framed run, not only when an artifact mode was requested")
- [x] Regression: a descendant that pauses after its claim and resumes across a second `u_frame_begin()` must not perturb the second run's counts
      - `UTEST: a stale emitter leaves the next run untouched` drives the three accounting events that shape really is -- run N admits the claim, run N's boundary drains and writes it off, run N+1 starts clean -- through the pure seam, since the live schedule (an emitter preempted between its claim and its klog write, across a frame rollover) is not constructible from a kernel test. Seven new kernel tests plus four host regressions in `scripts/test-tooling.sh`; execution proven with a planted sentinel assertion, not by watching the total move.
- [x] Commit: `"test: fence capture emitters that outlive their binary at the run boundary"`

**Test checkpoint:** two consecutive `test_usermode_run()` invocations with a forked descendant deliberately emitting across the boundary leave the second run's frame count, owner reconciliation and capture budget exactly as if the descendant had not existed; the stale emitter is reported against the run that produced it. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `scripts/debug/kernel/run-exec-tests.bat` / `bash scripts/test.sh SUITE=exec` for the eleven kernel-side tests (`UTEST: run seal outranks every other capture verdict`, `UTEST: a sealed claim consumes and latches nothing`, `UTEST: a capture claim reserves exactly when it draws`, `UTEST: pending counts capture records still owed`, `UTEST: pending never underflows on a late completion`, `UTEST: a stale emitter leaves the next run untouched`, `UTEST: closing a run writes off and ends its epoch`, `UTEST: an unsettled claim is reported fail-closed`, `UTEST: a late completion cannot cancel a live claim`, `UTEST: write-offs do not leak across three runs`, `UTEST: admission survives past the uint32 ceiling`, `UTEST: reap selects only this owner's descendants`), and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` for the six host fixtures (1158/1158 expected).

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped a three-stage run boundary in `src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c`: seal admission, drain outstanding claims, reap the descendant tree to a fixed point.
> - The seal is the fence, not the task walk: every emitter passes `u_capture_claim`, so one flag read under the existing lock also stops a latched write and a descendant forked mid-walk.
> - Reservations are monotonic with a saturating `u_capture_pending`, because a per-run reset underflows when a claim admitted in run N completes after run N+1 zeroed it.
> - Two new wire records: `[UTEST-CAPTURE-PENDING]` in the ORIGINATING run, and `[UTEST-CAPTURE-UNREAPED]`; `scripts/utest-capture.py` refuses on both, WHENEVER it runs -- §59 owns the fact that it does not on a default `test.sh`.
> - `TASK_EXIT_UTEST_REAPED` joins the reserved exit-reason block in `include/kernel/sched/task.h`; kernel-internal, so the user ABI export allowlist is untouched.
> - Scope boundary: an emitter unschedulable inside the drain budget stays possible, so the generation check remains the last line and the pending report names what it cost.

> **Verified:** 2026-08-01 | commit `79efcdd5` | 4/4 items | build OK | 28080 kernel + 17 user tests PASS, 1158 tooling PASS, lint 0 errors, release flavor (`KERNEL_TESTS=off`) builds OK
> **Accepted:** [M] the reap loop and the run-boundary wrappers have no assertions -- only the pure helpers are driven (reason: the loop needs a live fork tree the kernel test surface cannot build; the seam wants an injectable task-table view, which is its own design) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §56 (item: "Give the reap LOOP a seam, not just its selection predicate")
> **Accepted:** [H] the two run-boundary records refuse the run only where the capture model is built, and `scripts/test.sh` builds it only under `XML=1`/`JSON=1` (reason: pre-existing and identical for every capture refusal, and making reconciliation unconditional on the primary test gate can red currently-green runs -- an operator-visible policy change, not a section fix) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §59 (item: "Reconcile the capture model on every complete framed run, not only when an artifact mode was requested")
> **Accepted:** [M] the drain and the reap grace terminate on the monotonic clock alone, so a stalled clock yields forever (reason: the same shape predates this section in `u_wait_with_timeout`, which runs per binary and would wedge the launcher first, so bounding only the new pair leaves the hang reachable while implying a safety the file lacks; and a naive yield cap risks cutting a healthy drain short, which is the false refusal this section removed) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §58 (item: "Give every launcher wait a clock-independent escape, not just a deadline")
> **Accepted:** [H] a claim already past its generation check can still publish into the NEXT run's slice if it outlives the drain (reason: the reap kills and cleans every descendant at the end of its own binary and a killed task never resumes, so the only remaining entries are a descendant published after the reap's final census and an emitter stuck inside klog -- both owned below; closing it otherwise needs per-run framing tags or a klog interlock) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §56 (item: "Give the reap a state that fork publication honours, so a descendant cannot be published into a tree already being reaped")
> **Accepted:** [H] an emitter killed between its log call and its credit is reported lost though its record reached the wire -- RESOLVED by §57, which made the counter simultaneous with delivery rather than choosing a direction: klog now publishes a per-record acknowledgement and the capture claim settles from it, so the emitter's survival is no longer part of the accounting (reason it was accepted here: an exact answer needed a core-service change) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §57 (item: "Publish a delivery acknowledgement from klog at the point a record is actually on the wire")
> **Accepted:** [M] a descendant published in the instant after the reap's final census is missed by the walk (reason: its OUTPUT is already fenced by the owner stop latch set before any kill round, and the survivor count is reported rather than dropped; closing it means an interlock in scheduler fork publication, which is a scheduler change wanting its own review) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §56 (item: "Give the reap a state that fork publication honours, so a descendant cannot be published into a tree already being reaped")

## 50. Per-Child Loader Evidence: Ring-3 Entry and Stage Flags in the Child, Not in Globals

The launcher's never-ran-versus-failed classification rests on evidence that is not bound to the child it describes. Two flags live in process-wide globals and ring-3 entry is not recorded at all, so a timeout after frame publication is unclassifiable and a late store from a killed loader can be read as the NEXT binary's evidence. Both items want the same home -- a per-child record -- so they land together.

> [!NOTE]
> **Split out of §42 on 2026-07-31**, verbatim, with their original XREFs intact. §42 was filed as one section over two surfaces and `section-manifest.py` returned `SPLIT-RECOMMENDED (5 open items)` across 7 files; §42 keeps the host-tooling reap-grace half (no QEMU dependency) and this section keeps the kernel half. The seam is real rather than administrative: this half is a core-scheduler edit plus kernel-test state with SMP blast radius, and §42's is a shell-test assertion change. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §42 (item: "Replace the elapsed-seconds bound with a structural invariant that cannot be shed by truncation")

- [x] Record positive ring-3 ENTRY evidence per child, so a timeout after frame publication stops being ambiguous
      Shipped as TWO flags in `struct task_utest_loader`, because the design review showed one could not honestly carry both claims. `frame_adopted` is set by the scheduler at all three points `exec_pending` goes 1 -> 0 (`schedule_now`, `schedule`, `task_exec_take_pending_frame`), gated on an ACQUIRE load of the flag so a stale zero cannot drop the mark; `entered_user` is set from ring-3 syscall entry when the saved CS carries RPL 3.
      - **Why not one flag**: adoption only witnesses the frame being SELECTED -- TSS, CR3, GS, swapgs and the iretq all still follow -- so a bit named "entered user" set there would report a loader or scheduler fault as a hung user test. Codex design review, `[high]`.
      - **`exec_pending` still cannot serve, but NOT for the reason filed.** The section text (inherited from §37) said it is force-cleared after `EXEC_PENDING_STUCK_TICKS`; it is not -- that threshold only gates a WARN, and a never-adopted frame keeps `exec_pending == 1` indefinitely. The real disqualifier is that a ZERO conflates "adopted" with "no frame ever published", which are opposite verdicts. Caught by the post-commit kernel-quality audit.
      - **What it closes**: `u_record_verdict` now splits the old single ambiguous bucket -- a timeout that reached exec but was never adopted is a never-ran `ERROR`, one that WAS adopted stays a `FAIL`, and proven ring-3 entry outranks both.
      - Found by §37's round-7 adversarial review -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §37 (item: "Decided: a refusal belongs in JUnit `<error>`, and the accounting was extended to match")
- [x] Bind the loader-stage evidence to the CHILD rather than to two process-wide globals, so it cannot outlive its invocation
      All FIVE per-spawn file-scope statics are gone (`s_pending_test_path`, `s_pending_expect_digest`, `s_loader_stage_fault`, `s_loader_reached_exec`, `s_loader_identity_mismatch`), replaced by `struct task_utest_loader` inside `struct task`, KERNEL_TESTS-only and reset by every constructor but NOT by `task_exec` -- the loader sets `reached_exec` and then execs itself, so resetting there would erase the evidence as it becomes load-bearing.
      - **Inputs** are armed by `task_create_captured` before `num_tasks++` publishes the task, the same barrier the capture fields already used; **outputs** are written by the loader into its own slot and snapshotted by the launcher from the specific child being reaped, in the pre-`task_cleanup` window `u_report_snapshot` already uses.
      - **The comments now claim only what holds.** The old ones credited a release/acquire pair for cross-CPU safety the code never had; `num_tasks` is a plain global and supplies no memory-model edge. Ordering rests on BSP-only dispatch, which `task_utest_report_nonbsp_dispatch` now DETECTS (once, loudly) at exec adoption and loader entry instead of only asserting in prose.
      - **Ring-3 evidence fails safe rather than open**: the syscall-entry site resolves through the global `current_task` cursor, so off-BSP it suppresses the write instead of setting another child's flag. Adoption marking is handed its target explicitly and stays correct on any CPU.
      - Found by §37's round-10 adversarial review, widened by §47 -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §47 (item: "Bind the loader's per-spawn state to the spawned task rather than to file-scope globals, covering `s_pending_test_path`, the two stage flags and `s_pending_expect_digest` together")
- [/] Fail the RUN closed, not just loudly, when non-BSP dispatch is detected while loader evidence is being recorded
      BLOCKED on per-CPU dispatch existing at all: today APs park in `ap_entry()` and never call `schedule()`, so the condition is unreachable and a suite-wide latch would guard a state no run can enter. The detector reports once and the syscall path suppresses its write; that is the whole mitigation until the cursor moves.
      - Owner of the enabling work: `03-memory-concurrency/TODO-07-smp-phase2.md` §3 (item: "Per-CPU current-thread cursor: `thread_current()` from `g_rq[this_cpu()]`, not global `current_task`/`current_thread`"). When that lands, this becomes implementable and the suppression above becomes removable.
      - Raised by Codex adversarial round 2, `[medium]` -> XREF: `03-memory-concurrency/TODO-07-smp-phase2.md` §3 (item: "Per-CPU current-thread cursor: `thread_current()` from `g_rq[this_cpu()]`, not global `current_task`/`current_thread`")
- [x] Commit: `"test: bind loader-stage and ring-3-entry evidence to the child that produced it"`

**Test checkpoint:** a force-killed loader's late stage store cannot change the NEXT binary's ERROR-versus-FAIL classification, and a binary that publishes a frame then hangs in ring 3 is reported distinctly from one that never entered ring 3. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `scripts/debug/kernel/run-exec-tests.bat` / `bash scripts/test.sh SUITE=exec` for the five kernel-side tests (`UTEST: loader evidence cannot outlive its invocation`, `UTEST: loader evidence reset clears every field`, `UTEST: loader evidence snapshot copies every field`, `UTEST: ring-3 entry evidence requires an RPL-3 selector`, and the extended `UTEST: record verdict maps never-ran onto the artifacts`); full run `bash scripts/test.sh` -- 28109 kernel + 17 user-mode PASS, 0 failures, and `bash scripts/test-smoke-matrix.sh` 4/4 legs for the scheduler edit.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped `struct task_utest_loader` in `struct task` (KERNEL_TESTS-only), replacing all five per-spawn file-scope statics in `test_usermode.c`.
> - Inputs are armed by `task_create_captured` before publication; outputs are written by the loader into its own slot and snapshotted from the reaped child.
> - Two distinct flags, not one: `frame_adopted` at the three `exec_pending` adoption sites, `entered_user` at ring-3 syscall entry on CS RPL 3 -- adoption predicts entry, a CPL3 syscall proves it.
> - `u_record_verdict` splits the old ambiguous "reached exec then timed out" bucket into never-ran `ERROR` (never adopted) and `FAIL` (adopted).
> - The BSP-only invariant is now DETECTED one-shot at adoption and loader entry; the cursor-resolved ring-3 write suppresses itself off-BSP instead of contaminating another child.
> - Scope boundary: per-CPU cursor, off-CPU reap barrier and release/acquire publication are owned by `03-memory-concurrency/TODO-07` §3, reciprocally XREF'd.
> **Verified:** 2026-08-01 | commit `3dbd281b` + review fixes | 3/3 items (1 parked) | build OK both flavors | 28109 kernel + 17 user-mode PASS, 0 fail | smoke matrix 4/4 legs
> **Accepted:** [M] ring-3 evidence resolves through the global `current_task` cursor and can only suppress, not correctly bind, off-BSP (reason: needs a per-CPU current-task identity that does not exist yet) -> XREF: 03-memory-concurrency/TODO-07-smp-phase2.md §3 (item: "Per-CPU current-thread cursor: `thread_current()` from `g_rq[this_cpu()]`, not global `current_task`/`current_thread`" at line 120)
> **Accepted:** [M] the launcher reads the child TCB in a pre-`task_cleanup` window with no off-CPU death barrier (reason: pre-existing window already relied on by `u_report_snapshot`; barrier is scheduler-redesign scope) -> XREF: 03-memory-concurrency/TODO-07-smp-phase2.md §3 (item: "task_cleanup reap barrier: a TASK_DEAD task must be off-CPU on ALL CPUs before its lock-free frees" at line 126)
> **Accepted:** [M] constructor-armed loader inputs get program order on the BSP, not a C memory-model publication edge (reason: `num_tasks` is a plain global; a release/acquire protocol is scheduler-redesign scope) -> XREF: 03-memory-concurrency/TODO-07-smp-phase2.md §3 (item: "Tasks-publication lock: serialize `num_tasks++`/slot-publish vs scheduler enumeration" at line 134)
> **Accepted:** [M] a multi-threaded exec can separate "publication consumed" from "exec frame run", so `frame_adopted` over-reports there (reason: the clear-versus-consume semantics are an exec/scheduler design call; this section errs toward FAIL rather than changing them) -> XREF: 03-memory-concurrency/TODO-07-smp-phase2.md §3 (item: "Decide what an exec-frame publication means for a MULTI-THREADED task" at line 130)
> **Deferred:** [M] non-BSP dispatch during evidence recording is detected and contained but does not fail the RUN closed (reason: unreachable until APs dispatch; the enabling cursor work is the blocker) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §50 (item: "Fail the RUN closed, not just loudly, when non-BSP dispatch is detected while loader evidence is being recorded" at line 2110)
