## 51. A Reproducible Kernel Assertion Total, and the Intermittent TEB Halt Behind It

The suite's headline number is not reproducible: the SAME tree reports `PASS: 27971 kernel` on one run and `PASS: 27965 kernel` on the next, with zero failures either way. A total that moves on its own cannot be used as a regression signal, which is what every reader currently uses it for.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-08-01 from §44's verification. Measured on PRISTINE HEAD (`59c81443`, every §44 edit stashed): one run reported 27971 and five reported 27965 -- so the variance is HEAD's, not §44's. It was found only because §44 tried to explain a 6-assertion drop and would have been read as a regression by anyone who compared two totals; the doctrine that a drifting count is a finding rather than noise is the reason it is filed instead of rounded off.

- [x] Find which assertions vary and make the total a fact about the code rather than about the host's heap state
      - **The filed suspects were wrong, and knowing that took instrumentation.** The three exhaustion messages all come from `test_faultinject.exe` driving DELIBERATE, site-armed fault injection, so they are deterministic and were never the variance. Nothing in the runner could say otherwise: `test_runner.c` computed each suite's assertion delta and discarded it on the next line, so the sole artifact of a moving total was the total.
      - **Runner now emits a per-suite `[COUNT]` trace plus a `[COUNT-END]` trailer**, off by default and enabled by `test_quiet=2` (`TEST_QUIET_COUNT_TRACE`). `test_quiet` has always been read with `ascii_atoi`, so widening it from a flag to a 3-value enum adds no field and needs no `BOOT_INFO_VERSION` bump. Records go through `klog_unrated()`: the shared limiter drops above 100 msgs/sec and TEST escapes it today only because all 32 rate slots are consumed before its first message, which is tag ordering rather than a contract.
      - **New `scripts/test-count-stability.sh` is the consumer, and it refuses to compare an incomplete trace** -- ordinals contiguous from 1, no duplicates, no `trunc=1`, and a trailer whose `records=` matches what was counted. A truncated trace compares its surviving subset perfectly while the record that moved is the one missing, which reports "stable" and is worse than reporting nothing.
      - **Two real host-dependencies found and fixed, both by DESIGNED experiment rather than by waiting.** `--mem` varies guest RAM instead of hoping the variance recurs: `klibs: NtGetRandom` passed at `-m 2G` and FAILED with `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` at 3G and 4G, because its probe-accept path used a kernel STACK buffer and relied on it landing below `MM_USER_PROBE_ADDRESS` (0x7FFF0000); kernel stacks sit high in RAM, so that premise is a fact about the host. It now uses a scratch heap buffer (the heap is allocated low at boot) and ASSERTS the precondition instead of assuming it.
      - **The original 6-assertion swing was reproduced and named**: four `AVX2:` correctness suites in `test_cpu_security.c` gated on `simd_avx2_ok`, which is a DISPATCH flag that `hw_profile.c`'s boot-time auto-tune clears when the measured AVX2-over-SSE2 gain is small. That input is a wall-clock micro-benchmark on a loaded host, so the same machine answered differently run to run: 9 of 10 identical runs skipped those suites and the 10th ran them, moving the total by exactly 6 and the skip count by 4. Added `simd_avx2_capable` (set once by the feature probe, never cleared by tuning) and gated the correctness suites on it, so they ask whether the CPU CAN execute AVX2 rather than whether the dispatcher currently prefers it.
      - **Adjacent latent defect fixed in the same pass**: five assertions in `test_usermode_launcher.c` measured "records emitted" by differencing `klog_get_ring()`'s count, which SATURATES at `KLOG_RING_SIZE`, so once the ring filled every difference was 0 and the assertions silently verified nothing. The file's own comment already documented the hazard; the emission tests were written against the count anyway. They now derive the window from HEAD. Found because adding one log line per suite turned three of them red at once.
      - Evidence: `bash scripts/test-count-stability.sh --runs 10 --cross-mode` reports `STABLE -- 10 runs, 2704 suites each, 28133 assertions every time` and both modes agreeing; `--mem 2G,3G,4G,6G` reports STABLE across all four.
- [/] Reproduce and root-cause the intermittent boot halt observed in the same window, or prove it cannot recur
      - **Measured, not reproduced, and DEFERRED for root cause.** On this tree: 0 occurrences in 6 consecutive 2-CPU `SUITE=exec` runs (all exit 0) and 0 in the ~20 further 2-CPU full-suite runs this section ran for the count sweeps. That is a real downward revision of the ~1-in-3 rate the live-gotcha file recorded on 2026-07-31, and it is NOT proof the window is closed -- an unreproduced intermittent is exactly what the original filing warned against reading as absence.
      - The fix is an ABI ordering judgment on the TEB <-> `kernel_gs_base` handoff, which is operator-reserved, and it already has an owner carrying the same signature and the same reproduction method. The measurement above is recorded there rather than duplicated here. Note the evidence does NOT yet place the window on the AP path: every observation so far is `[cpu:0]` at a ring-3 entry, and the one 2026-07-29 sighting was a 1-CPU KVM leg, so "needs 2 CPUs" is not established either. Scoping it to AP ordering before the reproduction says so is the assumption the owning section's first item exists to prevent.
      -> XREF: `02-kernel-core/TODO-11-peb-teb-user-abi.md` §28 (item: "Reproduce deterministically before changing anything: identify what orders the TEB publish against the `KERNEL_GS_BASE` write on the AP path, and whether a tick landing between them is the window")
      - Observed ONCE in ~12 consecutive local runs: `[CRIT] sched: ring-3 task 4 thread 0 has TEB but kernel_gs_base=0 (task.teb=0x7ffdb000 thread.teb=0x7ffdb000)` followed by `[**] FATAL -- system halted`, on a 2-CPU KVM boot, at `test_smoke_boot.exe`.
      - **This is the exact signature `CLAUDE.md` records as the SMP regression the four-leg smoke matrix exists to catch**, so a single unreproduced occurrence is evidence the window is still reachable rather than evidence it is gone. It did not reproduce on the same tree, which is why it needs a deliberate hunt (repeat-count boots on TCG and KVM at 1 and 2 CPUs) rather than a fix guessed from one log.
      - Intermittency is the hazard: a halt that fires once per dozen runs is invisible to a single-shot gate and will be read as a flake by whoever next sees it.
      - **Observed THREE more times on 2026-08-01 during §47, with a lead the original filing did not have: it did not reproduce in isolation.** All three fired inside a full `SUITE=exec` run or a smoke-matrix leg, whereas 10 consecutive plain `SMOKE_SMP=1 test-smoke.sh` boots passed -- 5 on pristine HEAD and 5 on the §47 tree, built and run identically. So the trigger correlates with STATE the suite leaves behind (the same run logs carry `task_exec: OOM allocating argv address table`, `task_exec: OOM allocating private-frame table`, and `task_fork: per-process PML4 allocation failed for child slot 29`), not with booting per se
      - That points the hunt at allocator/task-slot pressure rather than at a pure SMP timing window, and it means a repeat-count sweep of BARE boots would measure the wrong thing -- the sweep has to run the suite first, or run the matrix repeatedly. It also ties this item to the assertion-total item above: both suspects are the same exhaustion paths
      - The §47 attribution check is worth keeping as the method: stash the section, build, boot N times, restore, build, boot N times. It is what separates "my diff broke the boot" from "I reproduced the filed bug" -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §47 (item: "Moved the timeout marker out of the `-(signum)` range: `UTEST_EXIT_TIMEOUT` (-6) is DELETED and replaced by `TASK_EXIT_UTEST_TIMEOUT`")
- [x] Commit: `"test: make the kernel assertion total reproducible and chase the intermittent TEB halt"`

> **Verified:** 2026-08-02 | commit `c56c93dd` | 2/3 items | build OK | 10-run + 4-RAM-size count sweep STABLE at 28151, cross-mode agrees on all four counters, 28151 kernel + 17 user-mode, 1169/1169 tooling, smoke 2.8s, lint 0 errors, KERNEL_TESTS=off builds
> **Deferred:** [M] the intermittent `ring-3 task has TEB but kernel_gs_base=0` halt is measured (0 in 6 consecutive 2-CPU `SUITE=exec` runs, 0 in ~20 further 2-CPU full-suite runs, against ~1-in-3 on 2026-07-31) but not root-caused; the fix is an operator-reserved ABI ordering call (reason: owner exists, evidence recorded there) -> XREF: `02-kernel-core/TODO-11-peb-teb-user-abi.md` §28 (item: "Reproduce deterministically before changing anything: identify what orders the TEB publish against the `KERNEL_GS_BASE` write, and whether a tick landing between them is the window" at line 699)
> **Quality reviewed:** 2026-08-02 | Codex 10x (design, adversarial, consistency, perf, re-adversarial x2, post-ship adversarial x2/consistency/perf) + Opus kernel audit | 1H+12M+6L fixed, 0 open | scope: kernel-code-quality

**Test checkpoint:** ten consecutive `bash scripts/test.sh QUIET=1` runs on an unchanged tree report the SAME kernel assertion total; a repeat-count boot sweep across TCG and KVM at 1 and 2 CPUs either reproduces the `kernel_gs_base=0` halt (which then gets a root cause) or bounds how often it can occur. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

## 52. Bind a Leg Pointer to the Record's Own Identity

`utest_pointer_pin_id` proves a pointer is byte-identical to what this script would have written for the leg its FILENAME names, and that the record it points at exists and is marker-committed complete. It never checks that the record was produced BY that leg. The record already knows: `test-run-identity.json` carries the leg, and the resolution algorithm derives the record path from the validated `run_id` -- but nothing compares the two.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-08-01 from §45's review-pipeline adversarial round, which rated it [high]. A `build/test-results-tcg-2cpu.run` holding the canonical emitter output for leg `tcg-2cpu` while naming a completed `kvm-2cpu` run id passes every check there is, so a consumer resolving the TCG leg reads the KVM leg's results -- and since §45, `utest_lease_acquire` will grant a lease over that record too, making the mis-binding durable rather than momentary. It is NOT a §45 defect and was deliberately not fixed there: the gap is in §38's pointer contract, it predates the lease, and closing it means validating a THIRD document (`utest-run-identity-v1`) inside the pin plus adding the same cross-check to the published consumer algorithm -- a new validation surface, not a lease edit. Not reachable from anything this script writes; it needs a hand-made or version-skewed pointer.

- [x] `utest_pointer_pin_id` now binds the record to the leg its pointer names: the marker must carry `identity: "test-run-identity.json"`, and `utest_identity_binds` then requires `run_id` and `leg` to match
      - The marker's own inventory authorises the read. `"identity": null` is a shape `utest-run-record-v1` legitimately emits, so a record whose marker disclaims an identity cannot be bound, and a `test-run-identity.json` planted beside such a record speaks for nothing.
      - `utest_identity_binds` validates POSITION, not substrings, which is what makes the binding sound. A document-wide test for `"leg": "<leg>"` reads values as keys -- `hostname` and `qemu` are emitted AFTER `leg`, so a host named `leg` would deny a valid record its pin -- and it is defeated in the other direction by a nested object or an escaped key spelling.
      - So the emitter's fixed layout IS the test: 14 lines, `{`/`}` alone at the ends, 12 two-space-indented keys in order with their commas, the three bound values literal and the other nine constrained to a scalar. A duplicate key, an extra key, a nested object and an escape all fail the walk.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §45 (item: "Implemented in `scripts/test.sh`: `utest_prune_records` honours live leases, and `utest_lease_acquire`/`utest_lease_release` ship the reader half")
- [x] The Step 0b identity write is a CHECKED prerequisite that refuses the run, found by this section's design review and shipped with it
      - Written as a bare `a && b` list, the write was exempt from `set -e`: errexit spares every command in an `&&` list but the last, so a failed write skipped the `mv` and the run carried on to publish a complete record whose marker said `identity: null` plus a leg pointer naming it -- exactly the record the pin now refuses, so the script would have minted pointers it would itself later reject.
- [x] `docs/testing/usermode-output-formats.md` requires the same binding in resolution step 3, and step 2b now routes a lease acquirer through steps 1-3
      - A lease is a durable hold, so acquiring one over an unbound pointer makes a cross-leg mis-resolution permanent rather than momentary. The record table also names the identity document, which it did not before.
- [x] The leg is validated against the PRODUCER alphabet before it is interpolated into either document, and pattern matching is pinned against an inherited `nocasematch`
      - A filename is not a grammar. `test-results-a","leg":"b.run` made the canonical emitters reconstruct `"leg": "a","leg":"b"` in both the pointer and the identity document, byte-equal to a hand-written pair, while a real parser sees a DUPLICATE `leg` and takes the second value -- so the injected pointer won a pin and spent budget a correctly-bound one needed. Every derived leg is `[a-z0-9-]`, so restricting the pin to that alphabet closes quoting, commas, globs and key injection together. The SWEEP deliberately does not get the check.
      - `nocasematch` is inheritable through `$BASHOPTS` from a non-bash parent, and it makes `*[!a-z0-9-]*` match case-insensitively -- so an uppercase leg stopped being rejected. Confirmed directly: `env BASHOPTS=nocasematch bash -c` accepts `UPPER`. `shopt -u nocasematch` now runs at the top of the script AND inside the validator, which is extracted and run standalone by the regressions.
- [x] Two parser-equivalence gaps closed from this section's adversarial round: `SMP_CPUS_SAFE` is normalised to canonical base-10, and no marker may contain a backslash
      - `cpus` is the one identity field emitted UNQUOTED. `SMP_CPUS=02` is digits-only and compares `-ge 1`, so it reached the emitter and published `"cpus": 02` -- a JSON parse error in the document whose entire purpose is to be read. The producer normalises it now, and the validator's numeric branch holds to JSON integer grammar rather than to "digits".
      - The marker's duplicate-key loop matches LITERAL keys, so an escaped second `identity` spelling placed after the required field was invisible to it while a parser decodes the escape and takes the last value. Every legitimate marker value is generated or charset-filtered, so rejecting the escape character outright closes that for all four keys at once.
- [x] Eleven regressions in the tooling suite, each with a positive control: cross-leg lease and pin budget, disclaimed identity, escaped marker key, injected legs, inherited `nocasematch`, and both parser bindings
      - The fixtures emit both the pointer and the identity through the PRODUCTION emitters (`utest_leg_pointer_doc_for`, the new `utest_identity_doc_for`), so neither document has a second spelling in the harness that could drift into passing what production rejects.
- [x] Commit: `"test: bind a leg pointer to the record's own identity"`

**Test checkpoint:** a byte-canonical `test-results-<A>.run` naming a complete record produced by leg `<B>` wins no pin, is refused a lease, and is reported as unresolvable rather than silently resolving to `<B>`'s documents; a correctly-bound pointer is unaffected. Test on: host tooling only (no QEMU dependency). Closed 2026-08-02: `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` -> `PASS 1180/1180`, including the eleven assertions above.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` | 1180/1180 pass 2026-08-02. New: `a pointer naming another leg's record is refused a lease`, `a cross-leg pointer pins nothing and does not spend the pin budget`, `an identity the marker disclaims cannot bind a record to a leg`, `an escaped duplicate identity key in a marker binds nothing`, `an identity a JSON parser rejects binds nothing`, `a pointer whose filename encodes leg '<X>' pins nothing` (x3), `the leg alphabet guard survives an inherited nocasematch`, `a leading-zero SMP_CPUS is normalised, never published as invalid JSON`, and `the documented canonical example matches the production emitter`. Host-only; no QEMU leg.

## 53. A Directory Occupying a Pointer or Lease Name

A directory standing where a file belongs is inert to every sweep and fatal to the writer. The pointer sweep enumerates with `find ... ! -type d`, so a directory named `test-results-<leg>.run` is never even a candidate; and `utest_publish_leg_pointer` finishes with `mv -fT`, which -- correctly, and for exactly the reason `-T` is there -- REFUSES rather than moving the pointer inside it. So that leg can never publish again, and nothing removes the thing blocking it. The lease reclaim has the same shape on `<record>/leases/*.lease`: section 46 gates directories out explicitly, because `rm` cannot remove one and detaching it into staging would only hide it.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-08-01 from section 46's consistency review, rated [medium]. It predates section 46 -- the `! -type d` filter and the `mv -fT` publication are both older -- and section 46 deliberately did not widen into it: that section's whole discipline was to close the DESTRUCTIVE half of a check-then-act race without inventing new destruction, and "remove a directory the harness did not create" is new destruction. The reviewer's framing is worth keeping: the two canonical verdict functions define what is DESTROYABLE, and directory policy sits upstream of them in BOTH namespaces, which is consistent but is a policy nobody has actually decided. Not reachable by accident today (nothing in the tree creates one), so it is robustness, not a live defect.

- [x] Decide the policy, and say it in one place rather than in two upstream filters
      - REPORT AND KEEP, stated once in `utest_blocking_dir` (`scripts/test.sh`): the single type test (`-d` plus `! -L`, because `-d` follows a symlink and a link to a directory is the sweep's business) plus a one-shot operator report. It replaces both upstream filters -- the pointer scan dropped its `! -type d` and now branches in-loop, and `utest_record_live_leases` calls the same predicate in its SILENT form because its stdout is its lease count.
      - `rmdir`-if-empty was rejected with `rm -rf`: an empty directory this harness did not create is still unknown provenance, and "empty" is a moment, not a property. Refusing the run was rejected too -- a directory under `leases/` blocks nothing, and one at a pointer name already produces a counted failure.
- [x] Make the diagnosis reach the operator, wherever the policy lands
      - Both ends of the pointer case now speak: the sweep names the path it cannot clear, and `utest_publish_leg_pointer` names the cause beside its existing counted failure. Reported on STDERR, which is load-bearing -- `utest_record_live_leases` returns its count through a command substitution, so a report on stdout would be read back as the lease count.
      - The section's original claim that the `mv -fT` refusal was "swallowed by `2>/dev/null`" was WRONG and is corrected here: that redirection binds to the preceding brace group, so `mv` printed a bare `mv: cannot overwrite directory` with nothing connecting it to the harness message. The gap was attribution, not suppression.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §46 (item: "Classify, parse and unlink through ONE verified object, so a replacement between the check and the action cannot be parsed or deleted in place of what was checked")
- [x] Cover both namespaces with one regression each: a directory at `test-results-<leg>.run` and one at `<record>/leases/<holder>.<id>.lease`
      - Eight landed, not two. p20 (pointer name: reported, whole, and a dangling pointer beside it still swept -- proving the widened scan kept its candidates); p5e extended to assert the publisher-side cause; L26 extended to pin the silent form and the still-exactly-1 count; L26b the age-out; L26c the `UTEST_LEASE_MAX=0` boundary; L26d the negative, that nothing a healthy run writes takes a hold; L26e the pre-delete recheck; L26f the staging namespace; L26g the `! -L` branch; L26h the two helpers at their boundaries.
      - MUTATION-VERIFIED, because a coverage review showed several assertions were green either way: deleting the pre-delete recheck fails L26e alone, and dropping `.*.tmp` from both call sites fails L26f alone. Both were restored and the suite re-run green.
      - Both new functions had to join the harness extraction lists (`UAR_LEASEFN`, `UAR_FNS`); the suite extracts production functions by name, so a new callee is a missing symbol until it is listed.
- [x] A glob argument is validated as a grammar, so no spelling can make a directory hold itself
      - `utest_hold_blocking_dirs "$dir" "" ""` expands to `$dir/`, which passes both halves of the type test, so the scanned directory counted itself and every record would take a permanent hold -- a fail-closed protection inverted into a fail-open one. Found by the test-coverage review and reproduced directly.
      - The first fix was a non-empty test, and the next adversarial round was right that this is not the same thing: whitespace-only word-splits to `$dir/` as well, a pattern merely CONTAINING whitespace splits into `$dir/` plus a pattern resolved against the working directory, `.` and `..` name the directory and its parent, and a `/` leaves the directory entirely. Each reproduced at 1 blocker. Rejected as a grammar now, with L26h asserting 0 for every spelling.
      - The same round caught L26g passing vacuously: both `ln -s` calls carried `|| true`, and every assertion is satisfiable with no links present, so the `! -L` branch could be reported covered without being reached. Setup is mandatory now and asserted inside the harness before the prune.
- [x] The caller's glob mode can no longer decide what retention is able to see
      - Post-commit review found the deepest one. Every retention loop enumerates with an unquoted glob, so an inherited `noglob` leaves each pattern literal and a blocking directory becomes INVISIBLE to the hold protecting it -- fail-open, from the environment alone, and the age cut then deletes its record recursively. `failglob` is the mirror: a healthy no-match aborts the shell and takes retention down on a tree that never held a lease. Measured at 1 blocker normally, 0 under `SHELLOPTS=noglob`, exit 1 under `BASHOPTS=failglob`.
      - Normalised with `set +f` and `shopt -u failglob nullglob dotglob` beside the `nocasematch` line that already existed for exactly this class of caller-supplied hole, so the pre-existing `*.lease`, `.*.tmp`, `.utest-sweep.*.d` and `test-results-*.run` loops are repaired too. L26i pins the switches, L26j the outcome under both modes; mutation-verified.
- [x] The last inline copy of the type test is gone, so the single definition is actually single
      - `utest_sweep_verified`'s restore path still asked `[ ! -d "$path" ]`, which follows a symlink: a symlink-to-directory newcomer read as an unremovable blocker, fell through to the unexplained branch, and its staging claim then held the record forever through the uncapped `U` path -- over an entry the sweep removes on sight. It asks `utest_blocking_dir` now.
- [x] A blocking directory HOLDS its record, because reporting it while the age cut deleted it was the policy contradicting itself
      - Found by design review before any code: the lease loop declining to unlink says nothing about `rm -rf -- "$RUNS_DIR/$old"` at the age cut, so the run could announce it was keeping the blocker and destroy it recursively in the same pass. Held by a new `D` tag unioned in AFTER the `UTEST_LEASE_MAX` truncation, exactly as the existing `U` (unresolved sweep claim) hold is, so a blocker can never evict a record holding a real lease.
      - Re-asked in the SILENT form immediately before the `rm -rf`, because classification and destruction are two loops and the writer of such a directory is by definition not participating in the retention mutex. This narrows the window to a syscall pair; it does not close it. Closing it means detaching the record before classifying it -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §62 (item: "Detach an age candidate before classifying it, so the record a hold protects cannot change between the verdict and the `rm -rf`")
- [x] The fail-closed holds no longer answer to a cap that was never theirs
      - The whole classification loop sat inside `if [ "$lease_max" -gt 0 ]`, so the documented `UTEST_LEASE_MAX=0` disable switch also switched off the `U` hold -- whose own comment says it must never consume a capped slot, which is the same argument for not being disabled by that cap. Only lease COUNTING stays inside the guard now; the sweep-claim reap and the directory hold run unconditionally. Zero disables admission and counting, which is what it is documented to do.
      - `.*.tmp` is held beside `*.lease`: the staging reaper unlinks with `rm -f`, which fails just as silently on a directory as the pre-46 lease loop did.
- [x] Commit: `"test: decide what a directory occupying a pointer or lease name means"`

**Test checkpoint:** a directory at a leg-pointer name produces a named diagnostic rather than silence, and the chosen policy is observable in a regression; a directory under `leases/` behaves identically to the pointer case; neither is removed by name, and one OBSERVED at classification holds its record through the age cut -- including past `UTEST_RECORD_KEEP`, at `UTEST_LEASE_MAX=0`, and under an inherited `noglob`/`failglob`. The check-then-delete race that can still destroy a blocker created after classification is NOT covered here and is owned by §62. Test on: host tooling only (no QEMU dependency).

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` -- p5e/p20 and L26/L26b-L26j under the run-record, leg-pointer and reader-lease groups; expect 1190/1190.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped `utest_blocking_dir` (single type test + one-shot stderr report) and `utest_hold_blocking_dirs` (count into `UTEST_DIRBLOCK_N`, mirroring `utest_sweep_reap`) in `scripts/test.sh`.
> - Wired into four call sites: the pointer sweep, `utest_record_live_leases` (silent), `utest_publish_leg_pointer`, and the prune's per-record hold pass.
> - A blocking directory OBSERVED at classification takes an uncapped `D` retention hold, so the age cut does not recursively delete what the run just said it kept.
> - The fail-closed `U` and `D` holds moved out from under the `UTEST_LEASE_MAX` guard; zero still disables lease admission and counting.
> - Inherited `noglob`/`failglob` are normalised at the top of `test.sh`, which also repairs every pre-existing retention glob.
> - Ten regressions, three of them mutation-verified against the guards they cover.
> - Canonical doc: the policy comment above `utest_blocking_dir` in `scripts/test.sh`, which states the limit as well as the policy.
> - Scope boundary: the classify-then-delete window is narrowed, not closed -- detaching the record first is §62.

## 54. A Write Abandoned Before Its First Chunk Ever Reached the Wire

Section 48 made every write() that reached the wire independently terminable, and stopped there by design. A write staging fewer than `UTEST_CAPTURE_CHUNK_MAX` raw bytes emits NOTHING until `test_usermode_capture_end()` flushes it, so a task killed inside the write loop leaves no record, no `wr` group, and nothing for the host to leave open -- the payload is simply gone and the artifact still claims byte-exactness for that owner. Both live callers (`src/kernel/sched/syscall.c:99-134`, `src/kernel/nt/nt_syscall.c:394-404`) reach the flush unconditionally on the normal path, so this needs a death inside the loop rather than a missing call.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-08-01 from section 48's design review, rated [high] there and accepted as a residual with this owner. The reviewer's fix -- emit an authenticated write-start record for every non-empty write -- is correct and is the only shape that makes a zero-record write visible, because visibility requires an emitted record. It was not taken inside section 48 because it roughly DOUBLES capture record traffic for the whole suite (each record is a ~350-byte klog line, and small writes dominate), which is a cost decision this section should make on measurements rather than one section 48 should make in passing. Section 48's item explicitly permitted either shape ("a per-write identifier (or explicit write-start/end records)").

- [x] Detect a write abandoned before its first chunk ever reached the wire
      - Measure first: the suite's current capture record count and serial wall-clock, against the same run with a write-start record per non-empty write. A cheaper alternative to weigh is a per-TASK "write open" flag set in `test_usermode_capture_start` and cleared in `test_usermode_capture_end`, with the abandoned case published once at reap instead of once per write -- it costs two plain stores per write and one record only when a write was actually abandoned.
      - Whichever shape wins, the host verdict already exists: an unclosed write is `capture_unterminated`, and an unarmed or lost channel is `capture_lost`. This is about producing the evidence, not about a new refusal vocabulary.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §48 (item: "Carry a per-write identifier (or explicit write-start/end records) so every write() call is INDEPENDENTLY terminable, and reconcile each write rather than only the owner")
- [x] Narrow the budget terminator's exemption from the OWNER to the write it actually cut
      - `[UTEST-CAPTURE-OVER]` carries owner/seq/scope/limit/charged but no `wr`, so `scripts/utest-capture.py` exempts EVERY unterminated write group of that owner once any budget stop fires -- including one truncated for an unrelated reason, which is exactly the masking `wr` was added to catch. Reachable: a captured fork descendant killed mid-write leaves an open group, and a later owner-budget stop on the same owner retroactively silences it. Found by section 48's kernel-quality audit, rated [medium].
      - Naming the cut write on the marker alone is NOT sufficient and was rejected during section 48's design review with evidence: the producer latches the whole owner, so a SECOND write open at that instant is neither named nor terminable and the run would refuse -- section 40's bounded stop turning back into a corruption verdict. The shape that closes both is one authenticated cut record per write open at the stop, which is the same machinery the item above needs; budget the wire reservation for it (TASK_MAX per owner) in one pass.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §48 (item: "Carry a per-write identifier (or explicit write-start/end records) so every write() call is INDEPENDENTLY terminable, and reconcile each write rather than only the owner")
- [x] Commit: `"test: make a write abandoned before its first chunk visible on the wire"`

**Test checkpoint:** a fixture in which a task dies inside its write loop with under one chunk staged is REFUSED rather than reconciled as a complete payload, and a run of ordinary short writes shows no regression in capture record count beyond the chosen design's stated cost. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Design note (the measurement the section demanded, taken before choosing):** one suite run emits **1,531** `[UTEST-CAPTURE]` chunk records across **1,427** distinct writes, mean **143.5** bytes/line, **219,745** of the log's 465,217 bytes. 1,330 of 1,427 writes (93.2%) emit exactly one chunk.
> - The reviewer's write-start-record shape therefore costs **+1,427 records (+93.2% capture traffic, +44% total serial bytes)** -- "roughly doubles" was right.
> - The per-thread evidence shape shipped here costs **zero records on the normal path** (two plain stores per write) and emits one only when a write was genuinely abandoned. Verified after the change: chunk records 1,531 and write groups 1,427, both unchanged, with 0 CUT and 0 ABANDON on a healthy run.
> - The section's own `~350-byte klog line` estimate was **2.4x over** the measured 143.5 bytes; the conclusion is unaffected, the figure is corrected here.
> **Notes:** the four design rounds rewrote this section's shape twice, and both reversals are the load-bearing part of the result.
> - The stop epoch exists because the budget latch stops the whole OWNER: naming only the write whose claim tripped it settles one write and strands every peer open at that instant, which is the masking `wr` was added to catch. Each write compares the owner's epoch against its own arm-time snapshot instead, so a peer parked mid-write settles itself without ever seeing a verdict.
> - The death outcome AND the owner's stop epoch are both snapshotted at the death transition, never re-read at reap. The launcher latches the reap fence before it kills, and a peer can exhaust the budget after a thread has already died -- so a reap reading either value later would relabel a genuinely lost write as an intentional teardown or an intentional cut, in both cases handing it the host exemption this section exists to withdraw.
> - Terminal records draw NO payload sequence number. The host authenticates an owner stop by `seq == limit` and by an unbroken chunk sequence, so a terminal record taking a number would break the shipped contract; they take the same lock and reserve against the same `admitted` counter instead, which is what puts them under seal+drain rather than a bare check-then-log.
> - The normal write path settles with a plain release store, not the compare-exchange: the only other party that settles a write is the reap barrier, which by construction runs after the thread is dead and off-CPU. Verified in the object code -- `test_usermode_capture_end`'s common path is `movb $0x2` then `retq`, with `lock cmpxchgb` only on the exceptional branch.
> **Verified:** 2026-08-02 | commit `358f6183` + review fixes | 3/3 items | build OK | tooling 1201/1201, 28171 kernel + 17 user PASS, lint 0 errors | capture wire byte-identical on the normal path (1,531 chunks, 1,427 groups)
> **Quality reviewed:** 2026-08-02 | Codex 7x (design x4, adversarial x2, consistency, perf) + kernel-quality-auditor + concurrency-evidence-mapper | 7H+6M+4L fixed, 0 open | scope: kernel-code-quality

## 55. Klog-Ring Assertions That Depend on How Much the Boot Logged

Three launcher tests bound their search of the klog ring with `count_after - count_before` from `klog_get_ring()`. That difference is not the number of entries added: `klog_ring_count` stops incrementing at `KLOG_RING_SIZE` (`src/kernel/klog.c:1220-1221`) while `klog_ring_head` keeps advancing and wrapping. Once the ring saturates the difference collapses to zero, so an assertion scoped to "entries added by this test" silently scopes to nothing -- and whether that happens is a function of how much the boot logged before the test ran, not of the behavior under test.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-08-01 from section 48's confirming adversarial round, rated [medium] there. This section's own design review then rejected the HEAD delta the filing prescribed, with evidence: head is modulo `KLOG_RING_SIZE`, so exactly one ring's worth of intervening entries also reads as zero appends, and past that the window covers only the post-wrap remainder. Both rejected shapes fail OPEN. The mechanism that shipped is the MONOTONIC klog sequence, which does not wrap, so an over-wide window is detectable and every caller fails closed on the sentinel.

- [x] Bound every ring window by the monotonic klog sequence, not by saturating occupancy
      - `test_capture_byte_and_flush_produce_the_wire_record`, `test_capture_exact_chunk_boundary_emits_one_final_record` and `test_capture_chunk_boundary_plus_one_emits_two_records` (`src/kernel/test/test_usermode_launcher.c`) are bounded by `u_test_scan_window` / `u_test_first_match` over a `klog_get_seq()` window and match on record CONTENT, so no assertion reads a fixed `head - N` offset that another CPU's line could shift.
      - `head_before` was the last residue of the rejected head-delta shape: it was written by six snapshot calls and read by none. Removed, and each site now takes the cheaper `klog_get_seq()` for the window's lower bound.
- [/] Prove the fix at the boundary the current tests cannot reach: a saturated ring
      - PARTIAL, and the partiality is the honest result. `test_capture_window_is_exact_under_a_saturated_ring` makes the claim whenever the run has genuinely saturated the ring and SKIPS explicitly when it has not. It originally saturated the ring itself; that was withdrawn on measurement (see the cost bullet below) and the remainder is owned by `02-kernel-core/TODO-04-system-logging.md` §16.
      - `test_ring_window_fails_closed_at_the_overrun_boundary` pins BOTH bounds where they diverge: the sized bound accepts an exactly-resident `KLOG_RING_SIZE` window (the kernel agrees -- `klog_lost_count()` reports 0 lost there), while the live bound refuses it because at that width the oldest entry sits on `head` and one concurrent append destroys it. Pure arithmetic, no emissions.
      - `test_ring_window_indexes_across_the_wrap` drives both scan helpers over a FIVE-slot synthetic ring with `head_after = 1`, so the modulo indexing is proven crossing index zero by construction rather than by whatever the run happened to log; slots outside the window are poisoned with matching payloads so reading one fails the test.
      - Five slots, not four, because 4 divides 2^32: with a power-of-two ring the wrapped subtraction a dropped `+ ring_size` produces lands on the SAME index, so a 4-slot fixture passes with the bug in place. Mutation-verified -- removing the term fails exactly the oldest-ordering and oldest-slot-read assertions.
      - The window ARITHMETIC is therefore proven exhaustively and at zero cost by the synthetic ring, which is what makes the live-ring proof affordable to leave opportunistic rather than forced.
      - `u_test_first_match` gained an `out_unreadable` out-param: it answered NULL for both a refused window and an honest absence, so a future negative assertion could have read "not emitted" off entries that had already been overwritten.
      - The exact global sequence delta is deliberately NOT asserted: `klog_ring_seq` is global, so `seq_after - seq_before == 1` would be a two-CPU flake -- the same defect class this section removes.
- [x] Repair the vacuous ring-count assertion this defect class left in the klog suite
      - `test_klog_ring_write` (`src/kernel/test/test_klog.c`) asserted `count_after >= count_before`, which is true for every possible execution including one where `klog()` dropped the entry. A sequence delta was no better (any CPU satisfies it), so it now scans its own sequence window for its own unique marker and asserts exactly one match.
      - That marker also moved off the shared `TEST` subsystem onto a private tag: `klog()` caps each subsystem at `KLOG_RATE_DEFAULT` per window and the runner spends that budget on its own assertion lines, so the marker could be legitimately dropped and fail the test over rate-window phase rather than ring insertion.
- [x] Commit: `"test: bound klog-ring assertions by monotonic sequence, not saturating occupancy"`

**Test checkpoint:** with the klog ring pre-saturated, every converted test still observes exactly the records its own emission produced; a record emitted by another CPU between the flush and the read does not change any verdict. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM (2 CPUs).

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped three TEST_CAT_EXEC tests (saturated-ring proof, overrun-refusal proof, synthetic cross-wrap indexing proof), removed the dead `head_before` state at six sites, and repaired the vacuous count assertion in `test_klog.c`.
> - All three drive the existing `u_test_ring_added` / `u_test_scan_window` / `u_test_first_match` helpers rather than adding a second window mechanism, so what is proven is the mechanism the other assertions actually use.
> - Deliberate saturation was MEASURED and withdrawn: 367 filler lines / 33,397 bytes / 1.09 s guest time on a full run, about 8.8 s on a real 38400-baud UART and ~24 s at a full shortfall against a 60 s harness deadline, and on SLOW boot media it evicts entries klog has batched but not yet flushed.
> - Corrected premise: the ring is NOT naturally saturated when these tests run, even in a full suite, so the live integration claim currently SKIPS rather than being manufactured; a sink-suppressed klog seam is what would make it cheap and deterministic.
> - The prescribed head delta was rejected at design review; the live bound now sits one entry tighter than the arithmetic allows because klog hands out the ring LIVE, and the copy-under-lock cure is filed as `02-kernel-core/TODO-04-system-logging.md` §16.

## 56. Fork Publication Interlocked With the Capture-Tree Reap

The run-boundary reap enumerates capture-owning descendants by walking `task_get_by_pid` until it returns NULL, and nothing stops a descendant publishing a NEW forked child in the instant after that walk passed its slot. `u_capture_reap_tree` (`src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c`) repeats the walk to a fixed point and takes a final census, which narrows the window to one publication racing one census, but a census cannot close it: the reap observes the task table, while `task_create_internal` publishes into it with `num_tasks++` under no protocol the reap participates in.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-08-01 from §49's adversarial review, rated [medium] there. §49 closed what it could without reaching into fork: the OWNER stop latch is set BEFORE any kill round, so a descendant published mid-reap inherits a latched channel and every claim it makes decides DROP -- its OUTPUT is fenced whether or not the reap ever sees it. What survives is a resource question, not a wire one, and §49 reports it (`[UTEST-CAPTURE-UNREAPED]`, which the host refuses on) rather than silently leaking. Closing it properly means an interlock in scheduler fork publication for a test-framework concern, which is a scheduler change and wants its own review. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §49 (item: "Reap or fence the whole CAPTURED DESCENDANT TREE at the run boundary, not just the top-level pid")

- [x] Give the reap a state that fork publication honours, so a descendant cannot be published into a tree already being reaped
      - The shape to evaluate: a per-owner "reaping" flag consulted by `task_create_internal` (`src/kernel/sched/task.c`) at the point it would inherit capture ownership, so a fork whose parent belongs to a tree under reap either fails or is published already latched and already counted. The alternative -- having the reap re-scan forever -- is what the round cap exists to refuse.
      - Whichever shape wins must not put a test-framework check on the general fork fast path for non-captured tasks; the inherit site is already conditional on the parent carrying capture, which is where the cost belongs.
- [x] Make the final census's survivor count exact rather than best-effort once the interlock exists
      - With publication interlocked, the count `u_capture_reap_tree` returns stops being a snapshot and becomes a statement, so `[UTEST-CAPTURE-UNREAPED]` can be tightened from "these were alive when we last looked" to "these are alive".
- [x] Regression: a descendant that forks during the reap's grace window is reaped or reported, never silently missed
      - Needs a fault-injection seam rather than a unit test: the kernel test surface cannot fork, and the window is one publication against one census.
- [x] Give the reap LOOP a seam, not just its selection predicate
      - §49 exported the pure helpers (`u_capture_decide`/`_apply`/`_pending`/`_close`/`_settle`/`_descendant_of`) and they are all driven, but `u_capture_reap_tree`, `u_capture_drain`, `u_capture_seal`, `u_capture_close_epoch` and `u_capture_inflight` have zero assertions -- each is reachable only from a live run. That is where the round cap, the grace shape, the kill/terminate ordering, the final census and the descending cleanup order all live.
      - This is not a theoretical gap: §49's own post-commit kernel audit found two defects (a grace wait that burned its full budget with no liveness re-check, and a cleanup pass that re-selected already-reaped descendants) and BOTH were inside that untested loop while every pure-helper assertion stayed green. Fixing them without a seam means the next regression there is equally invisible.
      - The shape to evaluate: lift the loop body over an injectable task-table view (enumerate / signal / terminate / cleanup as function pointers or a small vtable) so a test can drive a synthetic tree, or extend the fault-injection surface the framework already has.
- [/] Poison the rest of the BOOT after a degraded reap, not just the test-plan loop -- BLOCKED on a boot-policy decision (does a test-framework condition halt the machine?), owner: this section's follow-up, needs an attended call
      - Filed 2026-08-02 from §56's round-4 adversarial review, rated [high] there. `s_reap_degraded` aborts the plan loop, which closes the case the review named (the next `u_run_one` spawning into the contested slot). It does NOT cover what happens after `test_usermode_run()` RETURNS: `boot_phase3` goes on to `task_create(shell_loader_func, "cmd.exe")` (`src/kernel/main/boot_desktop.c:786`), and `task_create_internal` takes `pid = num_tasks` (`src/kernel/sched/task.c:833`) -- the same slot an unfinished `task_fork` chose at `child_pid = num_tasks` without publishing.
      - Why it is parked rather than fixed here: both available shapes are boot-policy changes, not test-framework changes. Returning a fatal from `test_usermode_run()` and halting means a test-infrastructure condition stops the machine before the desktop shell; making `task_create_internal` fail closed while poisoned means a test flag changes scheduler behaviour system-wide and refuses `cmd.exe`. Either is a deliberate, hard-to-reverse call that CLAUDE.md reserves for an attended decision.
      - Reachability, so the priority is honest: the path requires a task to still be inside `task_fork` after TWO 200 ms drains during which the launcher yields throughout. On single-global-`current_task` dispatch that means a forker preempted and never rescheduled across ~400 ms of yields. It is a forward-looking SMP guard, not an observed failure.
- [/] Carry the abort CAUSE into the machine artifacts, not only the human lines -- BLOCKED on the artifact-schema owner (§36 emission surface), reciprocal XREF below
      - Filed 2026-08-02 from the same review, rated [medium]. The guest announcement and TAP bail-out now name `reap degraded` vs `smoke failed`, and `scripts/test.sh` echoes the producer's cause instead of asserting "smoke gate". The JUnit `<error>` and the synthetic JSON error record still describe every abort with one fixed cause, so a degraded reap reaches CI's structured output looking like a smoke-gate failure.
      - XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §36 (the artifact-emission and byte-reconciliation section that owns the XML/JSON record shapes)
- [x] Commit: `"sched: interlock fork publication with the usermode capture-tree reap"`

**Test checkpoint:** a binary forking continuously through its own teardown leaves no capture-owning descendant alive and unreported after `u_run_one` returns; the survivor count on `[UTEST-CAPTURE-UNREAPED]` matches the tasks actually still live. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM (2 CPUs).

> **Notes:** the interlock is a lock-ORDERING statement, not a new lock: `pgroup_jobctl_lock` already covers `num_tasks++` at all three publication sites, so admission and publication order against the reap's latch for free.
> - Order is fence -> latch -> drain -> kill rounds -> exact census -> cleanup. Latch before drain (a growing tree never drains); drain before the FIRST kill (after one, a forker can die mid-constructor and be freed underneath).
> - Registration is a per-task `utest_capture_fork_inflight` COUNT the drain derives by scanning slots. A global counter strands on a killed forker; a per-task flag loses a second thread's fork (nothing gates `SYS_FORK` to thread 0).
> - Exactness needs both drained and unstranded: a registration on a task that died mid-constructor cannot be waited on but can still publish, so it downgrades the census rather than being ignored.
> - The census is scoped by a separate `utest_capture_fork_owner` that `task_utest_capture_unlink` does NOT clear. Slots are never reused within a boot, so an unscoped count would charge one mid-fork death to every later binary.
> - Either shortfall emits `[UTEST-CAPTURE-REAP-DEGRADED]` (new capture-family record; host refuses on it) rather than presenting a snapshot as a statement. Emitted even at `live=0`: an inexact zero reads identically to an exact one.
> - A degraded reap still KILLS but frees NOTHING -- not descendants, not the owner. A constructor the reap cannot account for holds pointers into those slots, so a bounded leak beats a use-after-free and the run is refused regardless.
> - It also ABORTS the suite (`s_reap_degraded`, sticky). An unfinished `task_fork` already claimed `child_pid = num_tasks` without publishing it, and the next `task_create_internal` takes that same slot -- so continuing is corruption, not a reporting gap.
> - The drain runs TWICE: once before the kills and once before the census. A task preempted inside `task_fork` only runs when the launcher yields, which it first does in the grace wait, so the second ask recovers the common case to exact.
> - Two items are PARKED, not done: poisoning the rest of the boot after a degraded reap (a boot-policy call), and carrying the abort cause into the JUnit/JSON records (§36 owns those shapes). Both are forward-looking SMP guards on an unreachable-today path.
> - Review asked for the kill rounds to be gated on the drain too. Not done: the kills are the only lever that restores progress, and hoisting the second drain ahead of them was measured to HANG the boot. The free stays gated; the residual is the parked item.

> **Verified:** 2026-08-02 | commit `05db7997` + this review commit | 3/5 items (2 parked) | build OK | 28257 kernel + 17 user-mode tests pass | 1205/1205 tooling | lint 0 errors | smoke matrix 4/4 (KVM+TCG at 1+2 CPUs)
> **Deferred:** [H] A degraded reap stops the test-plan loop but not the rest of the boot -- `boot_phase3` still spawns `cmd.exe` into the slot an unfinished `task_fork` claimed -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §56 (item: "Poison the rest of the BOOT after a degraded reap" at line 2369)
> **Deferred:** [M] JUnit `<error>` and the synthetic JSON error still describe every abort with one fixed cause, so a degraded reap reaches CI looking like a smoke-gate failure -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §56 (item: "Carry the abort CAUSE into the machine artifacts" at line 2373)
> **Accepted:** [M] The interlock is fork-only: `task_create_internal` (reachable via NtCreateProcess) publishes with no admission gate, so a captured binary can leave a non-capture-owning task the reap never selects -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §56 (item: "Poison the rest of the BOOT after a degraded reap" at line 2369)

> - The reap loop now takes its world as `struct utest_reap_ops`, so the round cap, grace shape, drain and cleanup order are driven by 10 assertions over a synthetic tree instead of only by a live run.
> - Non-captured forks pay one predicate on the parent's `utest_capture_active`; the whole interlock compiles out with `KERNEL_TESTS`.

## 57. A Klog Delivery Receipt, So a Record's Settlement Is Exact

The run boundary settles a capture record by crediting a counter after `utest_record_log` returns, and no placement of that credit is simultaneous with the record reaching the host: `klog_emit` reserves a ring slot under its own lock, releases it, then formats and calls `serial_write` (`src/kernel/klog.c`). An emitter killed between the log call and the credit therefore delivered its record and is still reported lost, so `[UTEST-CAPTURE-PENDING]` refuses a run whose output the host actually has.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-08-01 from §49's second confirming adversarial round, rated [high] there against the opposite arrangement. §49 chose the FAIL-CLOSED side of an unavoidable ambiguity and pinned it with a test: crediting BEFORE the emission would instead call a record delivered across klog's whole serial write, so a genuinely lost record produces a GREEN run -- a wider window in the worse direction. The narrow false-refusal that remains is what an exact receipt would remove, and removing it means klog publishing an acknowledgement at its own delivery boundary, which is a change to a core kernel service that every subsystem uses. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §49 (item: "Make a stale record's arrival DIAGNOSABLE rather than merely counted, so the run that inherits it is not the one that fails")

- [x] Publish a delivery acknowledgement from klog at the point a record is actually on the wire
      - `klog_receipted(level, subsystem, ack, cookie, fmt, ...)` plus the `klog_receipt_fn` typedef (`include/kernel/klog.h`); `klog_emit` (`src/kernel/klog.c`) carries `ack`/`cookie` and fires the acknowledgement on ALL THREE of its exits -- `delivered=1` immediately after `serial_write` and before the framebuffer and disk sinks, `delivered=0` on the verbosity-filter and rate-limit drops. Firing on every exit is what stops a declined record stranding its caller's obligation.
      - Identity is the caller's own `cookie`, so one claim is settled among many. Opt-in PER CALL rather than a registered sink: the ordinary path pays one register-resident NULL test, with no global load, no per-thread state and no extra lock on the line every subsystem emits. `klog()`/`klog_unrated()` pass `(NULL, 0)`.
      - The callback contract is stated on `klog_receipt_fn`: synchronous, bounded, safe with interrupts DISABLED (klog is callable from interrupt context and irqsave restores the CALLER's state), safe concurrently, and forbidden from calling klog at all -- a nested record reverses sink order against serial, and a nested receipted one recurses without bound.
- [x] Settle the capture claim against that acknowledgement instead of against the emitter's own credit
      - `u_capture_receipt` is the ack (`src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c`), carrying the claim's generation as the cookie; the four emitting sites use `utest_record_log_receipted` and the post-switch `u_capture_complete` is deleted, so settlement is a property of the EMISSION rather than of the emitter running again. The generation-mismatch early return keeps its direct credit because it emits no record.
      - The frame census moved onto the receipt too, counting only `delivered=1` AND only for a claim whose epoch is still live. Counting the attempt would declare a record the host never received, so a declined record would fail frame reconciliation first and make the run's own diagnosis unreachable behind generic corruption; counting a STRAGGLER whose epoch already closed would charge the NEXT run for a record that is not in its slice, and the host reads `records=` as an exact count, so an innocent successor would be refused for a stream it emitted correctly.
      - A declined record discharges the same debt but is tallied apart in `s_capture_undelivered` and reported as `[UTEST-CAPTURE-UNDELIVERED] run=N count=N`, read inside the close as one lock-protected transition so a late receipt cannot fall between the report and the epoch end. Host grammar plus a `capture_undelivered_records` refusal in `scripts/utest-capture.py`, with five `scripts/test-tooling.sh` vectors.
- [x] Regression: an emitter terminated between the log call and its credit leaves the run GREEN, not refused
      - `UTEST: a delivered record settles without its emitter` (`test_usermode_launcher.c`) drives the receipt and then stands for an emitter that never ran again: pending 0, forgiven 0. Siblings pin a declined record counted apart and a receipt from a closed epoch settling nothing.
      - `UTEST: a real emission settles through the live receipt` binds the PRODUCTION path -- a real capture write moves the live counters -- so a call site that stopped carrying the receipt fails. It saves and restores the capture run state around the drive, because the seal set at the last `u_frame_end` survives for the rest of the boot and would otherwise refuse the probe for a reason unrelated to what it asserts. `Klog: receipt acknowledges a delivered/declined record` pins the mechanism's ordering over klog itself, and `Klog: a NULL ack is the ordinary path` pins the opt-out branch so a regression that always passed the receipt struct cannot dereference a NULL callback on every ordinary line. Neither alone covers all of it; together they do.
      - §49's `UTEST: an unsettled claim is reported fail-closed` KEEPS its expectation. What it pinned was a chosen direction for an unavoidable ambiguity; the receipt removes the ambiguity, so what still reaches that close unsettled is a genuine loss and reporting it is simply correct.
- [x] Commit: `"klog: publish a delivery receipt so capture settlement is exact"`

**Test checkpoint:** a capture emitter killed after its record reaches serial but before it credits the claim leaves the run's pending count at zero and produces no `[UTEST-CAPTURE-PENDING]` record; ordinary klog callers pay no additional lock or callback per line. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `scripts/debug/kernel/run-boot-tests.bat` (klog receipt) and `scripts/debug/kernel/run-exec-tests.bat` (capture settlement); host grammar via `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh`. Expect 3267 boot + 2119 exec kernel tests and 1210 tooling tests, all passing (full suite 28283 kernel + 17 user-mode). The exec figure recorded at ship time was 2111 and was stale by 4: the review's own additions account for exactly +4 statically, so the shipped number never matched the tree it described.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped `klog_receipted()` + `klog_receipt_fn` (`include/kernel/klog.h`, `src/kernel/klog.c`): an opt-in per-record delivery acknowledgement fired on all three `klog_emit` exits, `delivered=1` right after `serial_write`.
> - Capture accounting settles from that receipt (`u_capture_receipt`), replacing the credit the emitter made after the log call; the frame census likewise counts delivered records.
> - A declined record discharges its claim but is tallied apart as `[UTEST-CAPTURE-UNDELIVERED]`, with host grammar and a `capture_undelivered_records` refusal in `scripts/utest-capture.py`.
> - Ordinary klog callers pay one pointer test per exit: no global sink, no per-thread state, no added lock on the line every subsystem emits. Collapsing the callback and cookie into one argument keeps the call within the six integer registers, verified in the release build (`klog` reaches `klog_emit` with no outgoing stack store); the receipt is still spilled once inside `klog_emit` because it must live until the delivery boundary, and that 16 bytes of frame is accepted rather than split away into a second copy of the kernel's hottest function.
> - The callback contract (synchronous, bounded, IRQ-disabled-safe, SMP-concurrent, no klog calls) is stated on `klog_receipt_fn` and satisfied by construction by the only ack in the tree.
> - Does NOT close the window between the serial write and the acknowledgement, it narrows it from the whole remainder of the emitter's life to a single call. Two things still fit in that call: the emitter can be preempted and reaped so the callback never runs, or the boundary can close the epoch on another CPU so the claim is refused by generation. Either way a record that IS on the wire is reported pending. Owned by §59, which has the stream and can tell a claimed-but-absent record from a claimed-and-present one.
> - The section title's "exact" is about the SETTLEMENT MECHANISM, not about the outcome: settlement became a property of the emission rather than of the emitter running again. Read as a claim that no record can be misreported, it would be false.
> - `delivered=1` is a statement about ONE sink. The receipt fires after serial and before the framebuffer draw and the synchronous disk append, so it says nothing about the disk sink -- the one that can actually fail. Early is the point for this consumer, whose channel IS serial; the header spells the boundary out so a later consumer cannot read `delivered=1` as "durable".

> **Accepted:** [M] the new NULL-ack regression scans the LIVE klog ring after the snapshot lock is released, so a concurrent append can overwrite the window mid-scan (reason: pre-existing shape shared with `test_klog_ring_write`; the cure is a locked copying API, not a per-test change) -> XREF: 02-kernel-core/TODO-04-system-logging.md §16 (item: "Give klog a snapshot API that copies a selected window under the lock" at line 469)
> **Accepted:** [M] klog has no bounded wait until the sinks reach a target sequence, so a caller can force a flush but never wait for its own record (reason: adjacent klog capability rather than the receipt's scope; identified against Linux `pr_flush` by the parity pass) -> XREF: 02-kernel-core/TODO-04-system-logging.md §17 (item: "Add a bounded wait for a target sequence across the sinks that have a cursor" at line 499)
> **Deferred:** [H] the receipt narrows but does not close the window between `serial_write` returning and the acknowledgement -- an emitter preempted inside that call and then reaped never runs the callback, and a concurrent epoch close refuses the claim by generation (reason: in-scope, and not closable producer-side without publishing inside serial's critical section or nesting the budget lock under the serial lock) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §59 (item: "Reconcile a `[UTEST-CAPTURE-PENDING]` against the record census before refusing, so a provisional report cannot refuse a run whose output the host has" at line 2468)
> **Deferred:** [H] `[UTEST-CAPTURE-UNDELIVERED]` is emitted but never READ on the default test path, because `scripts/test.sh` builds the capture model only under `XML=1`/`JSON=1` (reason: in-scope, pre-existing for every capture refusal, and making reconciliation unconditional on the primary gate is an operator-visible policy change) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md §59 (item: "Reconcile the capture model on every complete framed run, not only when an artifact mode was requested" at line 2472)

## 58. Launcher Waits Bounded Independently of the Monotonic Clock

Every bounded wait in the launcher terminates on `u_uptime_ms()` alone, which is `uptime_ns() / 1000000`. If monotonic time stops advancing, each of them yields forever and the boot never proceeds -- and each is documented as bounded, so the failure looks like a hang with no diagnostic rather than the timeout it was meant to be.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-08-01 from §49's re-adversarial round, rated [medium] there against the two waits that section added. It is deliberately scoped WIDER than those two: the same shape predates §49 at `src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c` in `u_wait_with_timeout` (the per-binary wait and its SIGKILL grace), which runs for every binary and would therefore wedge the launcher long before the run boundary is reached. Bounding only the newer pair would leave the hang exactly as reachable while implying a safety the file does not have. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §49 (item: "Reap or fence the whole CAPTURED DESCENDANT TREE at the run boundary, not just the top-level pid")

- [/] Give every launcher wait a clock-independent escape, not just a deadline
      - PARTIAL, and the residue is named rather than implied: the escape covers every stall mode in which the launcher still gets CPU back, which is all five sites against a frozen or derived-clock failure. It does NOT cover a dead periodic TICK combined with a non-cooperative ring-3 child: preemption dies with the clock, so the launcher never runs again to sample its own watchdog, and no user-mode-visible mechanism can close that. BLOCKED ON the LAPIC NMI timer. -> XREF: `01-boot-platform/TODO-23-boot-watchdog.md` §7 (item: "The usermode-test launcher needs a preemption source the dead TIMER cannot take with it")
      - Five sites, all now one helper: `u_bounded_wait` (`src/kernel/test/test_usermode.c`), exported for assertions as `test_usermode_bounded_wait`, over `struct utest_wait_ops {now_ms, now_ticks, wait}` and returning `enum utest_wait_end {READY, TIMEOUT, STALLED}`. It replaced the per-binary wait, its SIGKILL grace, `u_capture_drain`, `u_reap_drain`, and the reap's per-round kill grace.
      - The escape is a WATCHDOG on `mono_tsc_raw()` (new accessor, `src/kernel/time/mono_clock.c`), an independent free-running counter. Its ticks are converted using the MEASURED rate from the companion `mono_tsc_hz()` -- as measured, with no second multiplier -- falling back to `MONO_TSC_HZ_MAX` (the ceiling `mono_source_qualify` accepts, now a named constant) whenever TSC is not the ACTIVE source, which covers both a TSC that never qualified and one the drift watchdog later demoted for running off its measured rate. A `_Static_assert` binds the launcher's fallback rate to that ceiling: a constant picked from the fastest shipping part is not an upper bound at all, and one below the real rate burns the budget faster than real time and stalls a HEALTHY wait. Rate inference was designed first and REJECTED at design review with evidence: `uptime_ns()` resolves through `read_ns` to `mono_ns_coarse()`, whose PMTMR epoch is banked every `PMTMR_ADVANCE_TICKS` = 8 ticks and whose LAPIC path is one-tick granular, so a HEALTHY clock plateaus for tens of ms and nothing in the tree bounds the yields that fit inside one plateau.
      - `UTEST_WAIT_ITER_CEIL` sits below all three as a termination PROOF, reachable only when the deadline clock and the counter are both frozen -- not as the mechanism. `UTEST_WAIT_WATCHDOG_CAP_MS` is overflow hygiene ONLY and sits above anything `utest_timeout_ms` (a UINT16) can express: the invariant that outranks latency is that the watchdog expires LATER than the deadline it backs, and a cap chosen to fit the harness would end healthy 30 s WHPX runs. The harness relation is an operator contract instead, stated in `docs/testing/usermode-env-matrix.md` -- raising the per-binary timeout means raising `TIMEOUT` with it.
      - The budget is re-derived every iteration and only ever allowed to GROW. A rate snapshotted at entry goes stale when a drift demotion moves timekeeping off the TSC mid-wait; recomputing closes that, and keeping the maximum stops a shrinking budget from retroactively expiring a watchdog that had not expired.
- [x] Report a stall as a stall, distinctly from a timeout
      - `TASK_EXIT_UTEST_STALLED` (`include/kernel/sched/task.h`, reason 5, both `_Static_assert` lists extended), the `[UTEST-WAIT-STALLED] site=` framed record, `u_reason_stall` / `UTEST_RSNC_STALL` as the artifact reason instead of a fabricated `timeout after Nms`, `utest_reap_result.stalled`, and a sticky `s_wait_stalled` that aborts the suite with cause `clock stalled`.
      - The safety actions stay SHARED with the timeout path (SIGKILL, grace, force-terminate) so the child is still proven dead; only the reporting divides. Reporting a stall as neither would walk on to snapshot and free a child never established as dead.
      - Every wait promotes its OWN outcome, including the SIGKILL grace (site `binary-kill-grace`): a first wait that timed out honestly leaves the run unmarked, so a grace that discarded its stall would leave the fault with no trace at all. The run-boundary seal+drain is hoisted ahead of `suite_aborted` for the same reason -- at the terminator its stall would land after both artifacts were already serialized as a clean run.
      - The sticky flag is PER-RUN, not per-boot: `struct utest_run_latches` holds it and is zeroed at the framed-run boundary, because `test_usermode_run()` is documented safe to call repeatedly and a latch outliving its run would abort the next one while its explaining record sat in a different run's frame. `s_reap_degraded` deliberately stays BOOT-sticky and out of that struct: it says an unfinished fork constructor still owns a task slot, which starting a new run does not settle, so zeroing it would trade a corruption guard for tidiness. It is now also gated on real constructor evidence (`drain_pending || drain_stranded`) rather than on an inexact census, because a grace that gave up on a stopped clock withholds exactness too and would otherwise poison the next run with a collision that never existed.
- [x] Regression: a frozen-clock seam proves every wait terminates
      - Sixteen tests in `src/kernel/test/test_usermode_launcher.c` (TEST_CAT_EXEC): already-ready costs zero yields; a live clock reaches TIMEOUT at its deadline; a frozen ms clock reaches STALLED at exactly the watchdog floor; a clock that stops PART WAY through a wait still reaches STALLED; both clocks frozen and a missing `now_ticks` both reach STALLED at the iteration ceiling; a coarse clock plateauing 4000 yields reports READY, not a stall; the reap over a frozen clock refuses exactness and frees nothing while still killing; a healthy tree under a frozen clock stays exact; a retry drain never erases an earlier stall; the stall stamp is distinct from the timeout stamp; the run latch is zeroed at the run boundary; a wait whose counter runs at the fastest TSC boot qualification accepts still ends as an ordinary TIMEOUT; the budget arithmetic itself (floor, measured rate, zero-rate fallback, NULL world, and that the widest configurable timeout still outlives its own watchdog); a documented 30 s timeout ends as TIMEOUT; and a counter outrunning its declared rate escapes early, while the same counter under the fallback does not.
      - Plus `test_mono_tsc_raw_advances` in `src/kernel/test/test_time.c` (TEST_CAT_SCHED), which pins the one property the watchdog source is exported for: it advances while the CPU retires instructions, bounded so a dead counter fails rather than hangs.
- [x] Commit: `"test: bound every launcher wait independently of the monotonic clock"`

**Test checkpoint:** with `uptime_ns()` pinned to a constant, a permanently pending capture claim and a permanently live descendant both leave their waits within a bounded number of yields, the run still publishes its terminator, and the serial log names the stall rather than reporting an ordinary timeout. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test.sh SUITE=exec` (TEST_CAT_EXEC, the wait and reap regressions) and `bash scripts/test.sh SUITE=sched` (TEST_CAT_SCHED, the watchdog-source assertion); full `bash scripts/test.sh` 28326 kernel + 17 user-mode PASS.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped one bounded wait (`u_bounded_wait`, seam `test_usermode_bounded_wait`) behind all five launcher wait loops, escaping on a `mono_tsc_raw()` watchdog rather than on the deadline clock it is meant to outlive.
> - Integrates through `struct utest_wait_ops` and a `now_ticks` member on `struct utest_reap_ops`, so the reap's two waits and the synthetic reap tree share one clock pair.
> - Downstream: a stall now has its own exit reason, framed record, artifact reason string, SERIAL verdict and sticky per-run suite abort, and withholds the reap's irreversible free -- recovered exactness is deliberately not granted.
> - Canonical doc: `include/kernel/test/test_usermode.h` (why a millisecond deadline is not a bound, and why the escape is a watchdog rather than a yield count).
> - Scope boundary: bounds the LAUNCHER's waits only, and only against stalls where the launcher still gets CPU back; a dead tick plus a spinning ring-3 child needs the LAPIC NMI timer and is parked with its owner.
> - Nothing outside `src/kernel/test/` changed except the `mono_tsc_raw()` / `mono_tsc_hz()` accessors, the named TSC qualification band, and the new exit reason.

> **Deferred:** the first item ships PARTIAL. Every launcher wait now escapes a stopped or derived-clock stall through the TSC watchdog, with the full reporting, per-run abort and regression set landed; what remains is the one mode no user-mode-visible mechanism can reach, where the periodic tick dies and takes preemption with it while a non-cooperative ring-3 child holds the CPU. Blocked on an out-of-band preemption source. -> XREF: `01-boot-platform/TODO-23-boot-watchdog.md` §7 (item: "The usermode-test launcher needs a preemption source the dead TIMER cannot take with it")

## 59. Capture Reconciliation Runs on the Default Test Path, Not Only for Artifacts

Every host-side capture refusal -- `capture_unterminated`, `capture_malformed_record`, `capture_unbound_owner`, the budget-marker checks, and the two run-boundary records §49 added -- is produced by `scripts/utest-capture.py`, and `scripts/test.sh` runs that checker only inside a block gated on `XML=1` or `JSON=1`. Both default to 0, so a plain `bash scripts/test.sh` and the post-commit hook path never build the capture model at all. The producer's records still reach serial, but nothing reads them, and the launcher's own summary does not count a `LOG_WARN` record as a failure -- so a run can lose captured output, or leave capture-owning descendants alive, and still exit 0 on the repository's primary test path.

> [!NOTE]
> Filed 2026-08-01 from §49's post-commit adversarial review, rated [high] there. It is deliberately scoped to the GATE rather than to §49's two records: the reach problem is pre-existing and identical for every refusal this module can raise, so fixing it for two records would leave the same hole under the other six. It is also a change to the primary test gate -- making reconciliation unconditional can turn a currently-green run red on a latent refusal that has never been read -- which is an operator-visible policy decision rather than a section fix. §49 corrected its own claim instead of widening the gate unattended. -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §49 (item: "Make a stale record's arrival DIAGNOSABLE rather than merely counted, so the run that inherits it is not the one that fails")

- [ ] Reconcile a `[UTEST-CAPTURE-PENDING]` against the record census before refusing, so a provisional report cannot refuse a run whose output the host has
      - The producer cannot settle this alone. Section 57 moved capture settlement onto klog's delivery receipt, reducing the window from the whole remainder of the emitter's life to a single call after `serial_write` returns -- but not to zero. Two things still fit in that call: the emitter can be preempted and reaped so the callback never runs, or the boundary can close the epoch on another CPU so the claim is refused by generation. Either way a record that IS on the wire is reported pending. Closing it producer-side would mean publishing delivery inside serial's own critical section (coupling the UART driver to the test framework) or holding the budget lock across serial output, which the kernel forbids.
      - The host is the side that can settle it: it HAS the stream, so a pending report whose records are all present in the authenticated slice is provisional rather than a loss, and one with a genuine hole is not. Decide it alongside the "make a capture refusal fail the run" item below -- both are the same policy call about what the default path refuses on.
      - -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` section 57 (item: "Settle the capture claim against that acknowledgement instead of against the emitter's own credit")
- [ ] Reconcile the capture model on every complete framed run, not only when an artifact mode was requested
      - In `scripts/test.sh`, the slice + `utest-capture.py model` steps are inside `if [ "$HAS_XML" -eq 1 ] || [ "$JSON_MODE" -eq 1 ]`. Reconciliation should run whenever a complete framed run was sliced; only the ATTACHMENT of captured output to an artifact needs to stay conditional on the mode that asked for it.
      - Expect this to surface refusals nobody has been reading. Land it with a full run first and triage whatever it reports as real findings, because that backlog is the reason the gate is worth closing.
- [ ] Make a capture refusal fail the run, not just warn
      - `[UTEST-CAPTURE-PENDING]` and `[UTEST-CAPTURE-UNREAPED]` are emitted at `LOG_WARN` and do not move the launcher's pass/fail counters, so even with the model built, the exit status depends on the host checker's return being consulted. Decide deliberately which refusals are fatal to `test.sh` and wire that.
- [ ] Regression: each boundary marker makes a plain `bash scripts/test.sh` fail
      - A fixture proving the default path -- no `XML=1`, no `JSON=1` -- refuses on a planted marker is the only thing that keeps this closed.
      - `[UTEST-CAPTURE-UNDELIVERED]` (shipped by section 57) is one of the markers the fixture must cover. Its grammar and `capture_undelivered_records` refusal are already exercised by five `scripts/test-tooling.sh` vectors, but nothing on the DEFAULT test path reads them yet, which is the gap this section owns.
- [ ] `[UTEST-WAIT-STALLED]` is an unconditional host-side refusal, on the default path like the rest
      - Section 58 makes a stalled clock abort the suite in-kernel, so the launcher's own exit already carries it. The HOST has no gate on the marker at all, which matters for the one case the in-kernel abort cannot reach: a stall recorded by a run whose serial is being read after the fact, or by a leg whose exit status was lost. It belongs in the same default-path checker this section builds, not in a second one.
      -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-04-usermode-test-framework.md` §58 (item: "Report a stall as a stall, distinctly from a timeout")
- [ ] Commit: `"test: reconcile captured output on every framed run, not only for artifacts"`

**Test checkpoint:** a planted `[UTEST-CAPTURE-PENDING]` or `[UTEST-CAPTURE-UNREAPED]` in a complete framed run makes a plain `bash scripts/test.sh` exit non-zero and name the refusal; a clean run is unaffected and pays no artifact-building cost it did not ask for. Test on: QEMU TCG, QEMU KVM.
